Document Control
Volume 8 is the controlled Product Requirements Document for The Eye. It governs 
what the frozen Strategic Intelligence Operating System must enable people and 
organizations to accomplish, what product state it preserves, where human authority 
applies, how it behaves under failure, and what evidence is required for acceptance.
CONTROL VALUE
Product The Eye
Tagline The Operating System for Strategic Intelligence
Controlled document Volume 8 - Product Requirements Document
Edition 1.0
Issue date 2 August 2026
Status Controlled Product Requirements Baseline
Binding sources
Volume 0 - Product Constitution v1.0; Volume 3 - Technical Architecture v1.0; Volume 4 - 
Engineering Specification v1.0; Volume 5 - AI Architecture v1.0; Volume 6 - Infrastructure 
Architecture v1.0; Volume 7 - Data Platform v1.0
Primary audience
Board and executive product authorities, CPO, CTO, CIO, CDO, CISO, CAIO, chief strategy and 
intelligence officers, product and design leadership, principal architects, engineering, data and AI 
governance, security, privacy, operations, commercial leadership, implementation partners, 
assurance, and customer authorities
Normative scope
Product authority; customer contexts; personas; workspaces; observation; knowledge; memory; 
foresight; warnings; risk; opportunity; Digital Twins; scenarios; simulation; decisions; Executive 
Operating System; agents; trust; administration; operations; delivery; packaging; acceptance; 
governance
Downstream inheritance
Volume 9 - UI/UX Design System; Volume 10 - Investor Package where product claims are used; 
product domain specifications; design specifications; roadmaps; release plans; acceptance plans; 
operating procedures; customer implementation and validation artifacts
Normative language
SHALL, MUST, SHALL NOT, and MUST NOT are binding. MAY permits an implementation choice inside 
a frozen user outcome, product-state, human-authority, semantic-parity, failure, and evidence boundary. 
Requirements carry stable PR-CC-NNN identities. Conformance is scoped to exact capability, journey, 
role, tenant or domain, deployment profile, entitlement, product release, data and model versions, 
configuration, consequence class, test period, and accepting authorities.
CONTROLLING RULE
Volume 0 controls constitutional meaning. Volume 3 controls product architecture. Volume 4 
controls engineering realization. Volume 5 controls AI architecture. Volume 6 controls 
infrastructure. Volume 7 controls the Data Platform. Volume 8 controls user-visible product 
behavior and acceptance within those authorities. Volume 9 will control the visual and interaction 
design system without changing this product contract.

Product Requirements Charter
The Eye is specified as an operating system for strategic intelligence: a persistent 
system of work, state, authority, evidence, foresight, decisions, commitments, 
outcomes, and learning. Chat, dashboards, reports, and copilots may exist as 
interaction forms; none defines the product boundary.
PRODUCT COMMITMENT BINDING PRODUCT CONSEQUENCE
Observe everything authorized External and internal sources are governed through rights, purpose, coverage, freshness, 
quality, custody, and withdrawal contracts.
Understand before predicting Evidence, identity, time, truth state, contradiction, the Knowledge Graph, and Enterprise 
Memory remain explicit before foresight is decision-active.
Explain every recommendation Evidence, assumptions, method, alternatives, uncertainty, dissent, provenance, and expected 
effect are available at the point of use.
Humans decide AI recommends, forecasts, simulates, reasons, and explains; only eligible authenticated 
humans approve consequential decisions and commitments.
Learn continuously Decision Replay and outcome evidence create governed change proposals; prior state is 
never rewritten and no AI component silently self-modifies.
Remember permanently and 
lawfully
Enterprise Memory preserves institutional continuity under permission, purpose, retention, 
correction, export, and deletion controls.
Protect by design Identity, policy, privacy, sovereignty, security, audit, resilience, accessibility, and customer 
control are product behavior, not operational add-ons.
Enterprise first The same semantic product operates across roles, customers, consequence classes, SaaS, 
Private Cloud, On-Premise, disconnected, and air-gapped profiles.
Definition of product complete
• Every material user outcome resolves to a stable capability, workspace, canonical work object, 
accountable owner, human-authority rule, lifecycle, failure state, success metric, and acceptance 
unit.
• Every strategic product preserves exact evidence, versions, time, truth state, provenance, policy, 
uncertainty, correction, dissent, explanation, deployment, entitlement, and current quality state.
• Every consequential transition presents the eligible human with the exact decision digest, 
alternatives, explanation, limitations, intended effect, and durable non-repudiable record.
• Every accepted journey demonstrates accessibility, localization integrity, identity and tenant 
isolation, privacy, security, performance, degraded behavior, recovery, export, replay, support, and 
deployment parity.
• No successful screen, query, workflow, agent run, forecast, simulation, report, or API response is 
represented as decision-grade when authority, evidence, context, policy, quality, or reconciliation is 
incomplete.
CONSTITUTIONAL INHERITANCE  /  C-001  ·  C-002  ·  C-004  ·  C-005  ·  C-006  ·  C-035  ·  C-047  ·  C-049  ·  C-052

Executive Product Statement
Volume 8 converts the frozen architecture into a complete product contract: who The 
Eye serves, what each person must accomplish, what strategic state the product 
governs, how AI remains subordinate to human authority, how the system behaves 
under uncertainty and failure, and what evidence allows a capability to enter operation.
The product is organized around persistent workspaces and canonical work objects rather than chat 
sessions or dashboard pages. Observation creates governed evidence. Understanding resolves identity, 
relationships, time, truth state, contradiction, the Knowledge Graph, and Enterprise Memory. Foresight 
creates weak signals, warnings, probabilistic forecasts, scenarios, and reproducible simulations. Decision 
Intelligence assembles alternatives and explanation for a human authority. The Executive Operating 
System turns the result into commitments, operating cadence, outcomes, and Decision Replay. Agents 
perform bounded work through the same identities, policies, tools, evidence, review, and lifecycle controls 
as people and services. The product is complete only when this loop survives correction, attack, 
disconnection, provider substitution, deployment change, accessibility needs, and recovery without losing 
strategic meaning or accountability.
Figure 1. The Eye product operating model. SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise deliver the same Observe, Understand, Predict, 
Simulate, Decide, and Learn product semantics. The Knowledge Graph, Enterprise Memory, Digital Twins, evidence, temporal truth, 
provenance, and Decision Replay support the loop. AI recommends, forecasts, simulates, reasons, and explains; humans decide.
MISSION 
STAGE PRIMARY PRODUCT WORK DECISION-GRADE OUTCOME
Observe Sources, collection plans, research, documents, live 
events, enterprise systems, intake, coverage
Authorized evidence with custody, time, quality, rights, 
provenance, and known blind spots
Understand
Intelligence objects, identity, relationships, Knowledge 
Graph, Enterprise Memory, claims, timelines, analysis, 
correction
Connected temporal meaning with explicit truth state, 
contradiction, uncertainty, and correction
Predict Weak signals, warnings, risk, opportunity, domain 
intelligence, forecasts across six horizons
Probabilistic foresight with method, evidence, 
calibration, alternatives, and accountable ownership
Simulate Scenarios, Scenario Marketplace, simulation runs, Digital 
Twins, sensitivity, Strategy Graph
Plural reproducible future states and option 
consequences with validated limits
Decide Decision cases, recommendations, dissent, authority 
gates, decisions, commitments
Explainable non-repudiable human decision against an 
exact digest
Learn Decision Replay, outcome linkage, cautious attribution, 
lessons, governed change
Improved operating policy and capability without 
rewritten history or silent self-modification

PRODUCT THESIS
The Eye is not a destination for answers. It is the governed operating environment in which 
organizations build strategic understanding, maintain institutional state, examine multiple futures, 
make accountable decisions, execute commitments, and learn from outcomes while retaining 
control of their data and authority.

1  Product Authority, Scope, and Decision Rights
Product Authority, Scope, and Decision Rights establishes the governed product 
capability to establish the controlled product baseline, assign singular decision rights, 
and resolve scope or authority conflicts before implementation. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 2. Product Authority, Scope, and Decision Rights. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Establish the controlled product baseline, assign singular decision rights, and resolve scope or authority 
conflicts before implementation.
Primary actors Board, executive product authority, architecture authority, product leadership, engineering leadership, 
assurance, and customer technical authorities.
Controlled product state Baseline identity, capability scope, accountable owner, authority class, controlling volume, decision record, 
exception, effective time, and supersession.
Human authority AI and automation may prepare evidence and proposals; only the recorded human authority may approve 
product meaning, consequential scope, or institutional commitment.
Primary failure condition A capability, requirement, owner, or decision cannot resolve to an approved authority.
Continuity contract Hold admission or release, preserve the disputed state, expose affected scope, and route the conflict 
through the recorded authority chain.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-01-001
The Eye SHALL provide a governed, role-appropriate capability 
through which authorized users can establish the controlled product 
baseline, assign singular decision rights, and resolve scope or 
authority conflicts before implementation.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.
PR-01-002
The product SHALL persist, expose, version, and reconcile baseline 
identity, capability scope, accountable owner, authority class, 
controlling volume, decision record, exception, effective time, and 
supersession; identity, owner, effective time, status, provenance, and 
policy SHALL remain explicit.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.
PR-01-003
The product SHALL enforce this human-authority boundary: AI and 
automation may prepare evidence and proposals; only the recorded 
human authority may approve product meaning, consequential scope, 
or institutional commitment.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.
PR-01-004
SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise SHALL preserve identical 
outcome, object, human-authority, explanation, correction, export, 
replay, and failure semantics; operational variance SHALL be 
declared.
Journey + fault test
Fault trace, user-visible state, preserved 
work, audit evidence, recovered state 
digest, and reconciliation closure.

ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-01-005
When a capability, requirement, owner, or decision cannot resolve to 
an approved authority, the product SHALL hold admission or release, 
preserve the disputed state, expose affected scope, and route the 
conflict through the recorded authority chain; it SHALL NOT represent 
unavailable, stale, unapproved, unsupported, or incomplete state as 
current and complete.

Fault trace, user-visible state, preserved 
work, audit evidence, recovered state 
digest, and reconciliation closure.

release evidence for the exact capability version, journey, role, tenant 
or domain, deployment, entitlement, data and model versions, 
configuration, consequence class, and period.

Failure and degraded experience
PRODUCT CONDITION REQUIRED PRODUCT BEHAVIOR
A capability, requirement, owner, or 
decision cannot resolve to an 
approved authority.
Hold admission or release, preserve the disputed state, expose affected scope, and route the 
conflict through the recorded authority chain.
Identity, permission, purpose, policy, 
authority, approval, scope, or 
controlling version cannot be 
established.
Deny the consequential transition, preserve user work and context, expose the missing authority, 
and route accountable resolution.
A source, data product, model, 
agent, integration, network, or 
service dependency is stale, 
unavailable, indeterminate, or 
recovering.
Declare the product state and affected scope, constrain unsafe actions, retain evidence and 
checkpoints, and resume only after verified reconciliation.
Acceptance evidence
• End-to-end journey evidence demonstrates a complete capability inventory, authority matrix, signed baseline 
decision record, conflict tests, and release evidence.
• Positive, negative, stale-state, unauthorized-role, cross-tenant, correction, and replay fixtures prove the product and 
authority contract.
• One golden journey suite passes across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise with every permitted difference 
declared.
• A fault exercise proves the declared continuity response when a capability, requirement, owner, or decision cannot 
resolve to an approved authority, including visible state, audit, and recovery closure.
CHAPTER ACCEPTANCE AND INHERITANCE
Product Authority, Scope, and Decision Rights is conformant only when every PR requirement closes for the declared 
acceptance unit, human authority is enforced, and product state remains truthful through correction, degradation, recovery, 
export, and replay. Primary anchors - V3: Ch. 1, Ch. 2; V4: Ch. 1, Ch. 2; V5: Ch. 1, Ch. 2; V6: Ch. 1, Ch. 2; V7: Ch. 1, Ch. 
2. Full trace: Appendix N.

2  Product Principles and User Value Contract
Product Principles and User Value Contract establishes the governed product capability 
to operate the closed strategic loop from observation through learning without collapsing 
The Eye into a chatbot, dashboard, BI platform, copilot, or reporting tool. It preserves 
evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, 
and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 3. Product Principles and User Value Contract. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, 
human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, 
degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Operate the closed strategic loop from observation through learning without collapsing The Eye into a 
chatbot, dashboard, BI platform, copilot, or reporting tool.
Primary actors Executives, intelligence leaders, analysts, operators, product owners, governance authorities, and 
customer sponsors.
Controlled product state Mission stage, user objective, work object, evidence state, uncertainty, recommendation, human decision, 
outcome, and learning disposition.
Human authority The product shall improve human understanding and decision quality while preserving uncertainty, dissent, 
accountability, and the user's final authority.
Primary failure condition A workflow optimizes engagement, speed, or automation at the expense of truth, explanation, control, or 
strategic completeness.
Continuity contract Constrain the workflow, expose the violated principle, preserve user work, and require accountable product 
adjudication.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-02-001
The Eye SHALL provide a governed, role-appropriate capability 
through which authorized users can operate the closed strategic loop 
from observation through learning without collapsing The Eye into a 
chatbot, dashboard, BI platform, copilot, or reporting tool.

test
Versioned user-journey fixture with 
product state, evidence, telemetry, 
audit, accessibility, and accountable 
outcome.

The product SHALL persist, expose, version, and reconcile mission 
stage, user objective, work object, evidence state, uncertainty, 
recommendation, human decision, outcome, and learning disposition; 
identity, owner, effective time, status, provenance, and policy SHALL 
remain explicit.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.

product shall improve human understanding and decision quality while

authority.

named eligible authority, exact scope,

3  Customers, Tenants, and Operating Contexts
Customers, Tenants, and Operating Contexts establishes the governed product 
capability to configure governed customer domains and operating contexts without 
forking the universal product core or leaking state across tenants. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 4. Customers, Tenants, and Operating Contexts. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Configure governed customer domains and operating contexts without forking the universal product core or 
leaking state across tenants.
Primary actors Government, defence, Fortune 500, banking, healthcare, manufacturing, energy, logistics, telecom, retail, 
consulting, SME, partner, and platform administrators.
Controlled product state Customer, tenant, domain, organization, jurisdiction, deployment, data authority, operating context, 
specialization package, policy set, and lifecycle.
Human authority Each customer controls its data and authorized operating context; platform operators cannot silently 
assume customer business authority or broaden purpose.
Primary failure condition Tenant, domain, jurisdiction, ownership, or purpose is ambiguous or conflicts with an active context.
Continuity contract Deny cross-boundary use, retain the last valid context, make scope visibly indeterminate, and require 
customer-authority resolution.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-03-001
The Eye SHALL provide a governed, role-appropriate capability 
through which authorized users can configure governed customer 
domains and operating contexts without forking the universal product 
core or leaking state across tenants.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.
PR-03-002
The product SHALL persist, expose, version, and reconcile customer, 
tenant, domain, organization, jurisdiction, deployment, data authority, 
operating context, specialization package, policy set, and lifecycle;

4  Roles, Personas, and Human Authority Model
Roles, Personas, and Human Authority Model establishes the governed product 
capability to receive role-appropriate information, actions, explanations, and escalation 
paths while separation of duties and human decision sovereignty remain enforceable. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 5. Roles, Personas, and Human Authority Model. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Receive role-appropriate information, actions, explanations, and escalation paths while separation of 
duties and human decision sovereignty remain enforceable.
Primary actors Executives, decision authorities, intelligence leaders, analysts, investigators, planners, operators, 
reviewers, auditors, administrators, data stewards, model governors, and external collaborators.
Controlled product state Persona, role, authority grant, purpose, scope, task, approval class, delegation, recusal, session, and audit 
attribution.
Human authority Roles may shape access and workflow but may not transfer or infer consequential authority; approvals 
require an authenticated eligible person and exact decision digest.
Primary failure condition A user is ineligible, conflicted, unavailable, delegated outside policy, or cannot understand the decision 
package.
Continuity contract Block the consequential transition, preserve draft work, expose the missing authority, and escalate without 
substituting automation.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

separation of duties and human decision sovereignty remain 
enforceable.

recusal, session, and audit attribution; identity, owner, effective time, 
status, provenance, and policy SHALL remain explicit.
Control + accountable

5  Information Architecture and Workspace System
Information Architecture and Workspace System establishes the governed product 
capability to move through a coherent system of workspaces, work objects, queues, 
investigations, strategic packages, and actions without losing context or authority. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 6. Information Architecture and Workspace System. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Move through a coherent system of workspaces, work objects, queues, investigations, strategic packages, 
and actions without losing context or authority.
Primary actors All authorized users, workspace owners, product administrators, and customer domain administrators.
Controlled product state Workspace identity, purpose, audience, navigation position, active context, object selection, query, filter, 
view, draft, task, and return path.
Human authority Navigation may change presentation and focus but may not change canonical state, permission, decision 
meaning, or the identity of the active work object.
Primary failure condition Workspace context, object identity, permission, or navigation history becomes inconsistent or stale.
Continuity contract Restore the last verified context, mark stale projections, preserve unsaved work, and prevent action 
against an unintended object.

6  Product State, Lifecycle, and Work Object Model
Product State, Lifecycle, and Work Object Model establishes the governed product 
capability to create, review, approve, publish, supersede, withdraw, archive, export, 
replay, and retire every material work object through explicit lifecycle states. It preserves 
evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, 
and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 7. Product State, Lifecycle, and Work Object Model. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Create, review, approve, publish, supersede, withdraw, archive, export, replay, and retire every material 
work object through explicit lifecycle states.
Primary actors Product teams, workflow owners, users, administrators, auditors, support, and downstream integrators.
Controlled product state Object identity, type, owner, version, status, truth state, policy, task state, approval state, publication state, 
dependencies, timestamps, and lifecycle events.
Human authority Only the canonical owner may commit an authoritative transition; views, agents, models, imports, and 
projections submit proposals or commands through the owning contract.
Primary failure condition An object transition is invalid, duplicated, out of order, stale, partially committed, or cannot identify its 
owner.
Continuity contract Reject or compensate the transition, preserve the last authoritative version and attempted action, and 
expose the unresolved lifecycle.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-06-001
The Eye SHALL provide a governed, role-appropriate capability 
through which authorized users can create, review, approve, publish, 
supersede, withdraw, archive, export, replay, and retire every material 
work object through explicit lifecycle states.

test

product state, evidence, telemetry, 
audit, accessibility, and accountable 
outcome.

identity, type, owner, version, status, truth state, policy, task state, 
approval state, publication state, dependencies, timestamps, and 
lifecycle events; identity, owner, effective time, status, provenance, 
and policy SHALL remain explicit.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.

canonical owner may commit an authoritative transition; views,

commands through the owning contract.

7  Accessibility, Localization, and Global Readiness
Accessibility, Localization, and Global Readiness establishes the governed product 
capability to complete every material journey and understand evidence, uncertainty, 
alerts, recommendations, approvals, and product state without dependence on a single 
sensory, language, or interaction mode. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, 
accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic 
product across every deployment profile.
Figure 8. Accessibility, Localization, and Global Readiness. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Complete every material journey and understand evidence, uncertainty, alerts, recommendations, 
approvals, and product state without dependence on a single sensory, language, or interaction mode.
Primary actors Users with diverse visual, auditory, motor, cognitive, language, literacy, device, network, and assistive-
technology needs.
Controlled product state Locale, language, script, direction, timezone, units, number format, reading level, assistive preference, 
contrast mode, keyboard state, and translation provenance.
Human authority Localization and accommodation may adapt presentation but may not alter strategic meaning, legal effect, 
risk severity, confidence, temporal truth, or approval wording.
Primary failure condition Content, control, alert, explanation, or approval cannot be perceived, operated, understood, translated, or 
verified.
Continuity contract Provide an equivalent accessible path, identify any limitation before consequential use, preserve user 
progress, and prevent inaccessible approval.
Normative requirements

8  Product System Context and Cross-Volume 
Conformance
Product System Context and Cross-Volume Conformance establishes the governed 
product capability to trace each product capability from user outcome through canonical 
layer, service, AI method, infrastructure, data contract, release, deployment, and 
acceptance evidence. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer 
control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every 
deployment profile.
Figure 9. Product System Context and Cross-Volume Conformance. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Trace each product capability from user outcome through canonical layer, service, AI method, 
infrastructure, data contract, release, deployment, and acceptance evidence.
Primary actors Enterprise architects, product leaders, principal engineers, AI and data architects, security, operations, 
assurance, and customer authorities.

9  Source Registry and Collection Workspace
Source Registry and Collection Workspace establishes the governed product capability 
to register, evaluate, approve, configure, monitor, suspend, and retire every external, 
internal, physical, scientific, commercial, and custom source. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 10. Source Registry and Collection Workspace. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, 
human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, 
degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Register, evaluate, approve, configure, monitor, suspend, and retire every external, internal, physical, 
scientific, commercial, and custom source.
Primary actors Collection managers, source owners, analysts, legal and rights reviewers, data stewards, administrators, 
and customer authorities.
Controlled product state Source identity, owner, authority, rights, purpose, endpoint, credentials reference, coverage, schedule, 
freshness, cost, health, approval, and withdrawal.
Human authority Connectivity or public availability does not establish collection authority; a human source authority 
approves purpose, rights, scope, and customer obligations.
Primary failure condition A source lacks valid authority, rights, ownership, purpose, coverage, or withdrawal handling.
Continuity contract Block activation or suspend collection, preserve the last valid checkpoint, expose consumer impact, and 
route remediation.

10  Observation Operations and Source Coverage
Observation Operations and Source Coverage establishes the governed product 
capability to operate continuous observation against explicit priorities, coverage targets, 
freshness contracts, legal constraints, and source health. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 11. Observation Operations and Source Coverage. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Operate continuous observation against explicit priorities, coverage targets, freshness contracts, legal 
constraints, and source health.
Primary actors Observation operators, collection managers, intelligence leaders, domain analysts, data operations, and 
customer supervisors.
Controlled product state Observation plan, source set, collection run, coverage dimension, expected versus observed arrival, blind 
spot, freshness, quality, incident, and operator action.
Human authority Automation may schedule and recover collection inside approved contracts; humans set priorities, accept 
material blind spots, and authorize source substitution.
Primary failure condition Coverage falls below target, sources conflict or disappear, freshness expires, or collection is constrained 
by law, cost, capacity, or security.
Continuity contract Declare the blind spot and affected products, apply approved fallback sources, preserve evidence, and 
escalate consequence-specific decisions.

11  Search, Discovery, and Research Workspace
Search, Discovery, and Research Workspace establishes the governed product 
capability to formulate structured and natural-language research intents, discover 
relevant internal and external evidence, refine scope, save research state, and 
reproduce results. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, 
human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment 
profile.
Figure 12. Search, Discovery, and Research Workspace. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Formulate structured and natural-language research intents, discover relevant internal and external 
evidence, refine scope, save research state, and reproduce results.
Primary actors Analysts, researchers, investigators, executives, planners, and authorized collaborators.
Controlled product state Research intent, query, scope, filters, source set, result rank, evidence item, citation, saved search, alert 
subscription, and retrieval snapshot.
Human authority Search may rank and summarize evidence but may not represent relevance as truth or conceal policy, 
coverage, recency, uncertainty, or omitted-source limits.
Primary failure condition Retrieval is incomplete, stale, policy-filtered, contradictory, unavailable, or cannot reproduce the cited 
result set.
Continuity contract Label limitations and coverage, preserve the query snapshot, offer safe refinement, and prevent 
unsupported conclusion or citation.

12  Document and Media Intelligence
Document and Media Intelligence establishes the governed product capability to inspect 
original documents, images, audio, video, geospatial products, and scientific artifacts 
beside aligned extracted, translated, summarized, and enriched derivatives. It preserves 
evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, 
and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 13. Document and Media Intelligence. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, human 
authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, degraded 
behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Inspect original documents, images, audio, video, geospatial products, and scientific artifacts beside 
aligned extracted, translated, summarized, and enriched derivatives.
Primary actors Analysts, investigators, researchers, records teams, reviewers, and domain specialists.
Controlled product state Original object, page or segment, timestamp, region, derivative, extraction method, model version, 
language, alignment, quality, annotation, and citation.
Human authority Machine derivatives remain clearly separated from original evidence; humans may correct or approve 
interpretations without overwriting the source.
Primary failure condition Content is malicious, corrupt, unsupported, partially extracted, mistranslated, misaligned, or missing 
accessible alternatives.
Continuity contract Quarantine unsafe content, show derivative limitations, preserve the original and user annotations, and 
route controlled reprocessing or review.

13  Live Event, Stream, IoT, and Telemetry Experience
Live Event, Stream, IoT, and Telemetry Experience establishes the governed product 
capability to observe live events and telemetry with explicit time, sequence, location, 
source trust, quality, latency, gaps, and correlation into strategic context. It preserves 
evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, 
and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 14. Live Event, Stream, IoT, and Telemetry Experience. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Observe live events and telemetry with explicit time, sequence, location, source trust, quality, latency, 
gaps, and correlation into strategic context.
Primary actors Operations centers, field teams, intelligence analysts, cyber analysts, supply-chain teams, engineers, and 
incident commanders.
Controlled product state Stream, producer or device, observation, event time, receipt time, sequence, watermark, quality, trust, gap, 
correlation, alert, and replay position.
Human authority Operators may acknowledge, annotate, suppress under policy, and escalate signals; the interface cannot 
infer completeness or trusted device state from transport success.
Primary failure condition Streams overrun, reorder, duplicate, lose time quality, develop gaps, or originate from an untrusted 
producer.
Continuity contract Show the degraded interval and data loss, apply bounded backpressure or approved shedding, preserve 
checkpoints, and constrain downstream use.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-13-001
The Eye SHALL provide a governed, role-appropriate capability 
through which authorized users can observe live events and telemetry 
with explicit time, sequence, location, source trust, quality, latency, 
gaps, and correlation into strategic context.
End-to-end product 
test
Versioned user-journey fixture with 
product state, evidence, telemetry, 
audit, accessibility, and accountable 
outcome.
PR-13-002
The product SHALL persist, expose, version, and reconcile stream, 
producer or device, observation, event time, receipt time, sequence, 
watermark, quality, trust, gap, correlation, alert, and replay position; 
identity, owner, effective time, status, provenance, and policy SHALL 
remain explicit.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.
PR-13-003
The product SHALL enforce this human-authority boundary: operators 
may acknowledge, annotate, suppress under policy, and escalate 
signals; the interface cannot infer completeness or trusted device 
state from transport success.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.

develop gaps, or originate from an untrusted producer, including visible state, audit, and recovery closure.

14  Enterprise Systems and Internal Data Experience
Enterprise Systems and Internal Data Experience establishes the governed product 
capability to connect ERP, CRM, databases, internal documents, collaboration systems, 
APIs, files, and operational platforms with visible ownership, scope, reconciliation, and 
customer control. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, 
human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment 
profile.
Figure 15. Enterprise Systems and Internal Data Experience. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Connect ERP, CRM, databases, internal documents, collaboration systems, APIs, files, and operational 
platforms with visible ownership, scope, reconciliation, and customer control.
Primary actors Customer administrators, data owners, integration engineers, business-system owners, analysts, privacy, 
and support.
Controlled product state Connection, source system, credential reference, object scope, extraction mode, schema, watermark, 
permission, reconciliation, owner, incident, and deletion behavior.
Human authority Customer source systems retain business authority; The Eye does not broaden access, reinterpret 
deletion, or claim transactional completeness outside the integration contract.
Primary failure condition Permissions, schemas, business keys, transactions, watermarks, or reconciliation totals become invalid.
Continuity contract Stop the affected path, retain the last consistent watermark, expose incomplete scope, and reconcile with 
the customer source owner.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-14-001
The Eye SHALL provide a governed, role-appropriate capability 
through which authorized users can connect ERP, CRM, databases, 
internal documents, collaboration systems, APIs, files, and operational 
platforms with visible ownership, scope, reconciliation, and customer 
control.

test
Versioned user-journey fixture with 
product state, evidence, telemetry, 
audit, accessibility, and accountable 
outcome.

connection, source system, credential reference, object scope, 
extraction mode, schema, watermark, permission, reconciliation, 
owner, incident, and deletion behavior; identity, owner, effective time, 
status, provenance, and policy SHALL remain explicit.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.
PR-14-003
The product SHALL enforce this human-authority boundary: customer 
source systems retain business authority; The Eye does not broaden 
access, reinterpret deletion, or claim transactional completeness 
outside the integration contract.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.

15  Collection Plans, Watchlists, and Priorities
Collection Plans, Watchlists, and Priorities establishes the governed product capability 
to translate objectives, risks, hypotheses, entities, geographies, markets, competitors, 
and time horizons into governed collection plans and watchlists. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 16. Collection Plans, Watchlists, and Priorities. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, 
human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, 
degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Translate objectives, risks, hypotheses, entities, geographies, markets, competitors, and time horizons into 
governed collection plans and watchlists.
Primary actors Executives, intelligence leaders, collection managers, analysts, planners, domain teams, and authorized 
customer sponsors.
Controlled product state Plan identity, sponsor, objective, priority, scope, source set, query, cadence, budget, trigger, owner, 
review, expiry, and outcome linkage.
Human authority AI may recommend collection gaps and priorities; humans approve purpose, budget, sensitive targets, 
escalation thresholds, and material scope changes.
Primary failure condition A plan becomes stale, duplicative, unauthorized, over budget, unbounded, or disconnected from an active 
objective.
Continuity contract Pause or narrow execution, preserve prior results, expose the planning defect, and require sponsor review 
before continuation.
Normative requirements

remain explicit.
Control + accountable 
review

16  Intake Validation, Quarantine, and Evidence 
Review
Intake Validation, Quarantine, and Evidence Review establishes the governed product 
capability to review integrity, rights, schema, classification, provenance, quality, 
duplication, and safety before observed material becomes decision-active evidence. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 17. Intake Validation, Quarantine, and Evidence Review. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Review integrity, rights, schema, classification, provenance, quality, duplication, and safety before 
observed material becomes decision-active evidence.
Primary actors Collection operators, data stewards, security, legal, analysts, reviewers, and customer authorities.
Controlled product state Intake item, original bytes, digest, source contract, validation result, quarantine reason, review task, 
disposition, custody, correction, and admission receipt.
Human authority Automated checks may admit only low-risk items within an approved rule set; uncertain, sensitive, or 
consequential cases require an eligible human reviewer.
Primary failure condition Content is unsafe, unauthorized, corrupt, ambiguous, duplicated, policy-conflicting, or cannot preserve 
chain of custody.
Continuity contract Isolate it, prevent downstream use, retain immutable evidence and reason, and support controlled release, 
rejection, or reinspection.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-16-001
The Eye SHALL provide a governed, role-appropriate capability 
through which authorized users can review integrity, rights, schema, 
classification, provenance, quality, duplication, and safety before 
observed material becomes decision-active evidence.
Evidence closure 
review

record resolving release, configuration, 
versions, tests, conditions, and 
authorities.

item, original bytes, digest, source contract, validation result, 
quarantine reason, review task, disposition, custody, correction, and 
admission receipt; identity, owner, effective time, status, provenance, 
and policy SHALL remain explicit.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.
PR-16-003

automated checks may admit only low-risk items within an approved 
rule set; uncertain, sensitive, or consequential cases require an 
eligible human reviewer.

review

named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.

17  Intelligence Object and Evidence Experience
Intelligence Object and Evidence Experience establishes the governed product 
capability to inspect and act on canonical intelligence objects while retaining identity, 
version, time, truth state, provenance, policy, quality, correction, and lifecycle context. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 18. Intelligence Object and Evidence Experience. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Inspect and act on canonical intelligence objects while retaining identity, version, time, truth state, 
provenance, policy, quality, correction, and lifecycle context.
Primary actors Analysts, investigators, researchers, executives, reviewers, auditors, agents, and integrators.
Controlled product state Object identity, type, version, tenant, event and record time, truth state, confidence, provenance, 
classification, quality, owner, correction, and status.
Human authority Interpretation, annotation, assessment, and recommendation remain distinct objects linked to evidence; no 
user or model may overwrite historical evidence.
Primary failure condition An object is malformed, conflicting, missing required semantics, stale, withdrawn, or cannot resolve to 
evidence.
Continuity contract Block decision-active use, show the invalid or degraded state, preserve history, and route correction or 
stewardship.

correction or stewardship.

18  Entity, Identity, and Relationship Workbench
Entity, Identity, and Relationship Workbench establishes the governed product 
capability to resolve entities, aliases, duplicates, merges, splits, and relationships 
through evidence-backed proposals and accountable stewardship. It preserves 
evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, 
and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 19. Entity, Identity, and Relationship Workbench. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product

Product contract

Product outcome Resolve entities, aliases, duplicates, merges, splits, and relationships through evidence-backed proposals 
and accountable stewardship.

Controlled product state Entity, namespace, identifier, alias, candidate match, relationship, evidence, score, conflict, merge or split 
case, reviewer, and revision.
Human authority AI may propose identity and relationship changes with reasons; only authorized stewardship can commit 
material canonical merges, splits, or relationship adjudications.
Primary failure condition Identity is ambiguous, evidence conflicts, a merge would cross tenant or policy boundaries, or a committed 
resolution proves wrong.
Continuity contract Retain candidates separately, prevent unsafe propagation, expose uncertainty, and support non-
destructive reversal and impact review.

20  Enterprise Memory and Strategic Record
Enterprise Memory and Strategic Record establishes the governed product capability to 
capture, retrieve, connect, correct, retain, and govern institutional facts, decisions, 
rationales, commitments, outcomes, lessons, and context across time. It preserves 
evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, 
and one semantic product across every deployment profile.

administrators.
Controlled product state Memory item, source, owner, audience, validity, record time, truth state, policy, retention, related objective 
or decision, correction, access history, and lifecycle.
Human authority Memory assists continuity but cannot silently convert prior text, model output, or organizational belief into 
verified truth; humans govern strategic and sensitive records.

purpose.

lifecycle review.

21  Claims, Contradictions, Confidence, and 
Uncertainty
Claims, Contradictions, Confidence, and Uncertainty establishes the governed product 
capability to distinguish evidence, claims, inferences, assessments, hypotheses, 
synthetic outputs, recommendations, decisions, and corrections while preserving 
disagreement. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, 
human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment 
profile.
Figure 22. Claims, Contradictions, Confidence, and Uncertainty. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human

Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Distinguish evidence, claims, inferences, assessments, hypotheses, synthetic outputs, recommendations, 
decisions, and corrections while preserving disagreement.

Controlled product state Claim identity, proposition, truth state, evidence, source independence, confidence form, uncertainty, 
contradiction set, assessment, dissent, review, and status.
Human authority Models and analysts may assess claims but cannot erase dissent, fabricate precision, or promote an 
inference to fact without governed evidence and authority.
Primary failure condition Claims conflict, confidence is uncalibrated, sources are dependent, evidence is insufficient, or a prior 
assessment is withdrawn.

require review or abstention.

it SHALL NOT represent unavailable, stale, unapproved, unsupported, 
or incomplete state as current and complete.

24  Corrections, Withdrawals, Reconciliation, and 
Replay
Corrections, Withdrawals, Reconciliation, and Replay establishes the governed product 
capability to correct or withdraw evidence and derived intelligence without erasing prior 
state, then identify, constrain, rebuild, and reconcile every affected product. It preserves 
evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, 
and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 25. Corrections, Withdrawals, Reconciliation, and Replay. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Correct or withdraw evidence and derived intelligence without erasing prior state, then identify, constrain, 
rebuild, and reconcile every affected product.
Primary actors Source owners, analysts, data stewards, knowledge engineers, product owners, governance, auditors, and 
affected decision authorities.
Controlled product state Correction case, authority, target version, reason, replacement, affected lineage, consumer, projection, 
warning, recommendation, decision, acknowledgment, and closure.
Human authority Correction authority may change decision-active state but cannot alter what was historically available; 
affected humans determine whether prior decisions require review.
Primary failure condition A target is unresolved, propagation is incomplete, projections diverge, consumers fail to acknowledge, or 
replay cannot reconstruct prior state.
Continuity contract Keep the case open, visibly constrain affected products, preserve historical versions, and escalate 
unresolved decision impact.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

through which authorized users can correct or withdraw evidence and

rebuild, and reconcile every affected product.

25  Weak Signal Detection
Weak Signal Detection establishes the governed product capability to discover low-
frequency, low-confidence, cross-domain, or emerging patterns before they become 
obvious while preserving evidence and uncertainty. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, 
accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic 
product across every deployment profile.
Figure 26. Weak Signal Detection. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, human authority, 
primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, degraded behavior, 
and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Discover low-frequency, low-confidence, cross-domain, or emerging patterns before they become obvious 
while preserving evidence and uncertainty.
Primary actors Intelligence analysts, domain specialists, executives, risk and opportunity teams, forecasters, and 
authorized agents.
Controlled product state Signal, hypothesis, feature set, baseline, deviation, source diversity, confidence, novelty, persistence, 
relevance, analyst disposition, and outcome.
Human authority AI may nominate and rank weak signals; humans decide strategic relevance, escalation, collection 
changes, and incorporation into assessments.
Primary failure condition Signals are driven by source artifacts, correlated feeds, model drift, seasonal noise, or insufficient 
evidence.
Continuity contract Label the signal as tentative or invalid, expose contributing evidence and blind spots, suppress unsafe 
escalation, and retain evaluation history.

26  Early Warning System
Early Warning System establishes the governed product capability to convert governed 
indicators and weak signals into timely, severity-appropriate, explainable warnings with 
explicit owner, acknowledgment, action, expiry, and outcome. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 27. Early Warning System. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, human authority, 
primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, degraded behavior, 
and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Convert governed indicators and weak signals into timely, severity-appropriate, explainable warnings with 
explicit owner, acknowledgment, action, expiry, and outcome.
Primary actors Executives, watch officers, intelligence leaders, operations centers, risk owners, planners, and escalation 
authorities.
Controlled product state Warning, trigger, threshold, severity, confidence, evidence, affected objective, owner, recipients, 
acknowledgment, escalation, suppression, action, and closure.
Human authority AI may detect and recommend; humans approve material warning policies, consequence-specific 
escalation, suppression exceptions, and institutional action.
Primary failure condition A trigger is late, noisy, duplicated, policy-filtered, unowned, unacknowledged, or unsupported by current 
evidence.
Continuity contract Declare warning quality, deduplicate, escalate by policy, preserve non-delivery evidence, and prevent 
unsupported severity or closure.

27  Risk Intelligence
Risk Intelligence establishes the governed product capability to identify, assess, 
connect, prioritize, monitor, mitigate, and learn from strategic risks against objectives, 
capabilities, dependencies, and risk appetite. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, 
accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic 
product across every deployment profile.
Figure 28. Risk Intelligence. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, human authority, primary 
failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, degraded behavior, and 
acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Identify, assess, connect, prioritize, monitor, mitigate, and learn from strategic risks against objectives, 
capabilities, dependencies, and risk appetite.
Primary actors Enterprise risk, executives, business owners, compliance, security, finance, operations, analysts, and 
auditors.
Controlled product state Risk, category, cause, event, consequence, likelihood distribution, exposure, velocity, owner, appetite, 
control, mitigation, indicator, residual risk, and review.
Human authority AI may estimate and recommend; accountable human risk owners accept assessments, appetite 
decisions, mitigation commitments, and residual risk.
Primary failure condition Risk is unowned, double-counted, stale, based on invalid dependencies, outside appetite, or expressed 
with false precision.
Continuity contract Show uncertainty and ownership gaps, constrain aggregate scores, escalate breaches, and preserve 
competing assessments.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE

through which authorized users can identify, assess, connect,

test

outcome.

value claims.

32  Forecasting and Prediction Horizons
Forecasting and Prediction Horizons establishes the governed product capability to 
create, compare, monitor, calibrate, supersede, and consume probabilistic forecasts at 
30-day, 90-day, 180-day, 1-year, 3-year, and 5-year horizons. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 33. Forecasting and Prediction Horizons. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, 
human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, 
degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Create, compare, monitor, calibrate, supersede, and consume probabilistic forecasts at 30-day, 90-day, 
180-day, 1-year, 3-year, and 5-year horizons.
Primary actors Executives, forecasters, analysts, planners, risk and opportunity owners, model governors, and decision 
authorities.
Controlled product state Forecast, target, horizon, cutoff, evidence snapshot, method, model, assumptions, distribution, intervals, 
scenarios, calibration class, owner, review, and outcome.
Human authority AI and statistical methods produce forecasts; humans approve decision-active use, material assumptions, 
overrides, and actions derived from them.
Primary failure condition A horizon is unsupported, inputs are stale, calibration fails, assumptions change, models disagree

33  Scenario Intelligence Workspace
Scenario Intelligence Workspace establishes the governed product capability to 
construct multiple plausible futures with explicit drivers, uncertainties, assumptions, 
branch points, indicators, implications, and strategic options. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 34. Scenario Intelligence Workspace. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, human 
authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, degraded 
behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Construct multiple plausible futures with explicit drivers, uncertainties, assumptions, branch points, 
indicators, implications, and strategic options.
Primary actors Executives, strategists, planners, analysts, risk owners, opportunity owners, facilitators, and decision 
authorities.
Controlled product state Scenario set, scenario, driver, assumption, uncertainty, branch, narrative, quantitative state, indicator, 
implication, option, owner, version, and status.
Human authority AI may propose and elaborate scenarios; humans approve framing, plurality, assumptions, decision 
relevance, and use in strategic deliberation.
Primary failure condition Scenarios collapse to one forecast, omit material uncertainty, duplicate each other, contain inconsistent 
assumptions, or lose evidence links.
Continuity contract Keep scenarios distinct, expose the defect, prevent decision-active publication, and route reframing or 
review.

34  Scenario Marketplace
Scenario Marketplace establishes the governed product capability to publish, discover, 
evaluate, license, install, configure, execute, update, and retire governed scenario 
packages. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human 
decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 35. Scenario Marketplace. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, human authority, 
primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, degraded behavior, 
and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Publish, discover, evaluate, license, install, configure, execute, update, and retire governed scenario 
packages.
Primary actors Scenario authors, domain experts, customer administrators, marketplace governors, reviewers, strategists, 
and buyers.
Controlled product state Package identity, publisher, version, domain, ontology, inputs, assumptions, methods, dependencies, 
rights, price, evaluation, compatibility, signature, installation, and lifecycle.
Human authority Installation never grants decision authority or bypasses customer governance; customers approve 
package trust, data access, configuration, and decision-active use.
Primary failure condition A package is unsigned, incompatible, vulnerable, expired, misleading, unlicensed, or requests excessive 
data or capabilities.
Continuity contract Deny installation or execution, preserve installed-state evidence, notify affected users, and support rollback 
or removal.

35  Simulation Design and Run Control
Simulation Design and Run Control establishes the governed product capability to 
define, validate, execute, compare, pause, reproduce, and retire simulations against 
exact scenarios, Digital Twin states, methods, constraints, and seeds. It preserves 
evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, 
and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 36. Simulation Design and Run Control. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, human 
authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, degraded 
behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Define, validate, execute, compare, pause, reproduce, and retire simulations against exact scenarios, 
Digital Twin states, methods, constraints, and seeds.
Primary actors Simulation specialists, strategists, analysts, engineers, planners, model governors, reviewers, and decision 
authorities.
Controlled product state Simulation definition, scenario, twin snapshot, model, parameter, distribution, constraint, seed, run, 
checkpoint, output, sensitivity, validity, cost, and evidence.
Human authority AI and simulation engines execute bounded methods; humans approve model suitability, material 
assumptions, run budgets, validity, interpretation, and decision use.
Primary failure condition Inputs are incompatible, constraints fail, runs diverge, resources exhaust, outputs cannot reproduce, or 
model validity is indeterminate.
Continuity contract Stop or quarantine the run, preserve checkpoints and partial outputs as non-decision-active, expose limits, 
and require review.

36  Digital Twin Portfolio and State
Digital Twin Portfolio and State establishes the governed product capability to manage 
living Digital Twins for company, competitors, supply chain, products, markets, 
regulations, infrastructure, processes, and organization. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 37. Digital Twin Portfolio and State. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, human 
authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, degraded 
behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Manage living Digital Twins for company, competitors, supply chain, products, markets, regulations, 
infrastructure, processes, and organization.
Primary actors Executives, operators, planners, engineers, analysts, domain owners, twin modelers, and decision 
authorities.
Controlled product state Twin identity, type, owner, scope, observed snapshot, synthetic branch, model, assumptions, 
synchronization, quality, validation, scenario use, version, and retirement.
Human authority Observed state and synthetic branches remain visibly separate; humans approve twin scope, validity, 
strategic use, and any action based on simulated state.
Primary failure condition A twin drifts, loses synchronization, mixes observed and synthetic state, exceeds validated scope, or 
cannot reproduce a prior branch.
Continuity contract Mark the twin degraded, prohibit unsupported decision use, preserve versions, and route resynchronization 
or revalidation.

38  Decision Intelligence and Recommendation 
Review
Decision Intelligence and Recommendation Review establishes the governed product 
capability to assemble and compare decision options with objectives, evidence, 
assumptions, forecasts, scenarios, simulations, risks, opportunities, trade-offs, dissent, 
confidence, and recommended actions. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, 
accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic 
product across every deployment profile.
Figure 39. Decision Intelligence and Recommendation Review. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Assemble and compare decision options with objectives, evidence, assumptions, forecasts, scenarios, 
simulations, risks, opportunities, trade-offs, dissent, confidence, and recommended actions.
Primary actors Executives, decision authorities, analysts, strategists, risk owners, opportunity owners, reviewers, and 
governance.
Controlled product state Decision case, question, options, objectives, constraints, evidence snapshot, assumptions, forecast, 
scenario, simulation, risk, opportunity, recommendation, explanation, dissent, and review.
Human authority AI recommends and explains but cannot approve, commit, or conceal alternatives; the eligible human 
decision authority owns the final choice.
Primary failure condition The package is incomplete, evidence is stale, explanation is unsupported, alternatives are missing, 
conflicts are undisclosed, or the recommendation exceeds authorized scope.
Continuity contract Block decision-ready status, expose the gap, preserve drafts and dissent, and require remediation or 
explicit human deferral.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-38-001

recommended actions.

39  Human Approval, Commitment, and Decision 
Record
Human Approval, Commitment, and Decision Record establishes the governed product 
capability to review the exact decision digest, approve or reject options, record rationale 
and conditions, create commitments, and preserve non-repudiable institutional 
accountability. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, 
human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment 
profile.
Figure 40. Human Approval, Commitment, and Decision Record. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Review the exact decision digest, approve or reject options, record rationale and conditions, create 
commitments, and preserve non-repudiable institutional accountability.
Primary actors Authenticated decision authorities, delegated approvers, secretariats, legal, finance, risk, governance, 
auditors, and commitment owners.
Controlled product state Decision identity, authority, digest, option, rationale, dissent, conditions, approval, signature, effective time, 
commitment, owner, deadline, audit, and supersession.
Human authority Only an eligible authenticated human can decide; AI, workflow, administrators, delegated services, and 
marketplace packages cannot synthesize or proxy approval.
Primary failure condition Authority is stale or conflicted, digest changes, required reviewers are absent, approval is ambiguous, or 
commitment creation partially fails.
Continuity contract Invalidate or hold the transition, preserve the attempted decision and evidence, require re-review, and 
reconcile commitments before closure.
Normative requirements

40  Decision Replay, Outcome Attribution, and 
Learning
Decision Replay, Outcome Attribution, and Learning establishes the governed product 
capability to reconstruct decision-time knowledge, compare expected and actual 
outcomes, attribute contributions cautiously, record lessons, and govern changes 
without rewriting history. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer 
control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every 
deployment profile.
Figure 41. Decision Replay, Outcome Attribution, and Learning. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Reconstruct decision-time knowledge, compare expected and actual outcomes, attribute contributions 
cautiously, record lessons, and govern changes without rewriting history.
Primary actors Decision authorities, executives, analysts, governance, auditors, model owners, product owners, and 
organizational learning teams.
Controlled product state Decision snapshot, evidence and model versions, recommendation, human rationale, commitment, 
intervention, outcome, counterfactual, attribution, lesson, change proposal, approval, and replay.
Human authority Learning may recommend product, policy, process, or model changes; authorized humans approve each 
change and prior decisions remain immutable.
Primary failure condition Decision-time state cannot reconstruct, outcomes are missing or confounded, attribution is overstated, or 
learning would cause silent self-modification.
Continuity contract Mark the replay incomplete, separate observation from inference, prevent automatic change, and route 
evidence collection or governance review.
Normative requirements

43  Strategic Health Score
Strategic Health Score establishes the governed product capability to understand 
decomposable strategic health across objectives, capabilities, risks, opportunities, 
execution, resilience, and learning without hiding uncertainty in a single score. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.

Controlled product state Score, dimension, indicator, measure, source, formula, weight, threshold, confidence, coverage, trend, 
decomposition, owner, exception, and version.
Human authority AI may compute and explain; humans approve dimensions, measures, weights, thresholds, exceptions, 
and any decisions based on the score.
Primary failure condition Inputs are stale or missing, weights are disputed, metrics are gamed, confidence is low, or aggregation 
masks a critical failure.

comparison, and route measure governance.

44  Proactive Intelligence and Priority Queue
Proactive Intelligence and Priority Queue establishes the governed product capability to 
receive a governed queue of material warnings, decisions, reviews, opportunities, 
collection gaps, commitments, and exceptions before manual inquiry. It preserves 
evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, 
and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 45. Proactive Intelligence and Priority Queue. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, 
human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, 
degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION

commitments, and exceptions before manual inquiry.

authorized agents.
Controlled product state Queue item, type, trigger, evidence, relevance, severity, urgency, confidence, owner, audience, rank, due 
time, action, acknowledgment, suppression, and outcome.
Human authority AI may nominate and rank; humans set policy, accept priority, suppress with reason, assign ownership, 
and decide consequential action.
Primary failure condition The queue becomes noisy, biased, duplicated, stale, unowned, overloaded, or hides a severe lower-
ranked item.
Continuity contract Declare queue quality, deduplicate and rebalance by policy, expose ranking reasons, escalate unowned 
items, and preserve suppressed history.

46  Collaboration, Tasks, and Workflow Orchestration
Collaboration, Tasks, and Workflow Orchestration establishes the governed product 
capability to coordinate durable human and machine work with assignments, 
discussions, evidence, deadlines, approvals, escalations, handoffs, and accountable 
completion. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human

Controlled product state Workspace, participant, role, thread, task, dependency, artifact, mention, deadline, approval, escalation, 
handoff, completion evidence, and audit.
Human authority Collaboration cannot broaden access or decision rights; machine work remains attributable, side effects 
remain gated, and eligible humans own approvals.
Primary failure condition Participants lose access, tasks duplicate or stall, handoffs lose context, messages cross domains, or 
workflows partially complete.
Continuity contract Constrain access, preserve committed work and evidence, expose stalled or residual state, and route 
reassignment or recovery.

own approvals.

review
