47  Reporting, Publishing, and Distribution
Reporting, Publishing, and Distribution establishes the governed product capability to 
create governed reports and intelligence products from canonical state, approve exact 
content, publish to authorized channels, track receipts, correct, withdraw, and archive. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 48. Reporting, Publishing, and Distribution. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, 
human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, 
degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Create governed reports and intelligence products from canonical state, approve exact content, publish to 
authorized channels, track receipts, correct, withdraw, and archive.
Primary actors Analysts, briefing authors, executives, operations, compliance, external-affairs teams, administrators, and 
recipients.
Controlled product state Publication, template, source snapshot, content, audience, classification, format, accessibility, approval 
digest, channel, schedule, receipt, correction, withdrawal, and archive.
Human authority Reporting is an output of the operating system, not its product boundary; humans approve material 
publication, audience, and classification.
Primary failure condition Content is stale or unsupported, formatting changes meaning, delivery exceeds audience, export loses 
controls, or corrections fail to propagate.
Continuity contract Block or withdraw distribution, preserve approved bytes and receipts, notify affected recipients, and require 
controlled republishing.
Normative requirements

48  Mobile, Field, Offline, and Disconnected Operation
Mobile, Field, Offline, and Disconnected Operation establishes the governed product 
capability to continue authorized strategic work on constrained devices and networks 
with explicit freshness, local protection, queued effects, synchronization, and conflict 
resolution. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human 
decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 49. Mobile, Field, Offline, and Disconnected Operation. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Continue authorized strategic work on constrained devices and networks with explicit freshness, local 
protection, queued effects, synchronization, and conflict resolution.
Primary actors Executives, commanders, field analysts, investigators, inspectors, operators, responders, remote teams, 
and administrators.
Controlled product state Device, user, local scope, cached object, watermark, offline package, queued command, conflict, 
synchronization, receipt, remote wipe, and expiry.
Human authority Offline access and effects are limited by pre-authorized scope; consequential decisions and queued 
commands retain human attribution and may require online revalidation.
Primary failure condition Connectivity is absent, local data expires, devices are lost or compromised, queues conflict, 
synchronization is partial, or remote authority changes.
Continuity contract Enter the declared offline mode, protect and expire local state, preserve queued work, prevent unsafe 
effects, and reconcile on reconnect.

49  Agent Operating Model and Marketplace
Agent Operating Model and Marketplace establishes the governed product capability to 
discover, evaluate, approve, install, configure, invoke, monitor, update, suspend, and 
retire governed agents as bounded product capabilities. It preserves evidence, 
uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one 
semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 50. Agent Operating Model and Marketplace. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, 
human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, 
degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Discover, evaluate, approve, install, configure, invoke, monitor, update, suspend, and retire governed 
agents as bounded product capabilities.
Primary actors Agent governors, product owners, administrators, developers, security, model risk, domain owners, 
marketplace publishers, and users.
Controlled product state Agent identity, role, package, publisher, version, tools, data scopes, model policy, permissions, budgets, 
evaluation, approval, installation, run, incident, and lifecycle.
Human authority Agents never acquire human decision authority; customer and platform authorities approve packages, 
permissions, deployment, use cases, and consequential side-effect classes.
Primary failure condition An agent is untrusted, incompatible, over-privileged, unevaluated, compromised, expired, drifting, or 
requests undeclared capabilities.
Continuity contract Deny installation or execution, isolate active runs, preserve evidence, notify affected users, and support 
rollback or retirement.
Normative requirements
ID REQUIREMENT VERIFICATION REQUIRED EVIDENCE
PR-49-001
The Eye SHALL provide a governed, role-appropriate capability 
through which authorized users can discover, evaluate, approve, 
install, configure, invoke, monitor, update, suspend, and retire 
governed agents as bounded product capabilities.

test

product state, evidence, telemetry, 
audit, accessibility, and accountable 
outcome.

identity, role, package, publisher, version, tools, data scopes, model 
policy, permissions, budgets, evaluation, approval, installation, run, 
incident, and lifecycle; identity, owner, effective time, status, 
provenance, and policy SHALL remain explicit.
Control + accountable 
review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.
PR-49-003
The product SHALL enforce this human-authority boundary: agents 
never acquire human decision authority; customer and platform 
authorities approve packages, permissions, deployment, use cases, 
and consequential side-effect classes.

review
Positive and negative role fixtures plus 
named eligible authority, exact scope, 
decision digest, and audit record.

to delegate bounded source observation, crawling, search, collection, and coverage

52  Knowledge Graph, Memory, and Reasoning 
Agents
Knowledge Graph, Memory, and Reasoning Agents establishes the governed product 
capability to propose graph curation, retrieve institutional memory, construct evidence-
grounded reasoning chains, surface contradiction, and preserve dissent and 
provenance. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human 
decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 53. Knowledge Graph, Memory, and Reasoning Agents. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Propose graph curation, retrieve institutional memory, construct evidence-grounded reasoning chains, 
surface contradiction, and preserve dissent and provenance.
Primary actors Analysts, knowledge engineers, memory stewards, investigators, decision teams, agent supervisors, and 
governance.
Controlled product state Reasoning task, context manifest, graph revision, memory snapshot, claims, evidence, inference, 
uncertainty, alternatives, dissent, proposal, reviewer, and outcome.
Human authority Reasoning agents may advise and propose but cannot declare derived claims as fact, mutate canonical 
graph or memory without authority, or suppress disagreement.
Primary failure condition Context is incomplete or policy-filtered, reasoning is circular, citations fail, memory is stale, or claims 
exceed evidence.
Continuity contract Abstain or return a visibly partial result, preserve the reasoning trace, constrain downstream use, and 
escalate review.

• Positive, negative, stale-state, unauthorized-role, cross-tenant, correction, and replay fixtures prove the product and 
authority contract.
• One golden journey suite passes across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise with every permitted difference 
declared.
• A fault exercise proves the declared continuity response when plans loop or expand scope, agents deadlock, tasks 
duplicate, supervision fails, context diverges, or briefing synthesis hides material dissent, including visible state, 
audit, and recovery closure.

56  Agent Controls, Explainability, Escalation, and 
Human Override
Agent Controls, Explainability, Escalation, and Human Override establishes the 
governed product capability to understand why an agent acted, inspect evidence and 
tools, constrain or stop execution, challenge outputs, escalate exceptions, and override 
within recorded authority. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer 
control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every 
deployment profile.
Figure 57. Agent Controls, Explainability, Escalation, and Human Override. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, 
controlled product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with 
explicit human authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Understand why an agent acted, inspect evidence and tools, constrain or stop execution, challenge 
outputs, escalate exceptions, and override within recorded authority.
Primary actors All users of agents, decision authorities, agent governors, security, model risk, operations, auditors, and 
administrators.
Controlled product state Agent identity, instruction, plan, context, model, tool call, permission, budget, action, result, explanation, 
refusal, escalation, override, residual effect, and audit.
Human authority Humans may pause, cancel, reject, override, or require review within their authority; administrators cannot 
erase evidence or convert operational control into business approval.
Primary failure condition Explanations are incomplete, tools exceed scope, side effects are uncertain, stop signals fail, escalation is 
unavailable, or override creates residual effects.
Continuity contract Fail closed for new consequential actions, isolate the run, preserve full trace, expose residual state, and 
require accountable remediation.

57  Trust and Provenance Experience
Trust and Provenance Experience establishes the governed product capability to verify 
source, chain of custody, transformation, model or agent, policy, review, approval, 
release, delivery, correction, and current trust state for every material product. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 58. Trust and Provenance Experience. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, human 
authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, degraded 
behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Verify source, chain of custody, transformation, model or agent, policy, review, approval, release, delivery, 
correction, and current trust state for every material product.
Primary actors All users, reviewers, auditors, administrators, investigators, customer authorities, and external recipients.
Controlled product state Trust statement, provenance chain, source, actor, method, artifact, digest, policy decision, approval, 
release, receipt, correction, exception, and verification status.
Human authority Trust signals explain evidence and controls but do not certify truth beyond their scope; humans interpret 
consequence and decide whether evidence is sufficient.
Primary failure condition Provenance breaks, signatures fail, artifacts mismatch, authority expires, corrections are pending, or 
verification services are unavailable.
Continuity contract Mark trust indeterminate, prevent unsupported consequential use, preserve the failing evidence, and route 
verification or investigation.

58  Identity, Access, and Tenant Administration
Identity, Access, and Tenant Administration establishes the governed product capability 
to federate identities, provision roles, scope domains, enforce least privilege, manage 
privileged access, review entitlements, and revoke access across deployments. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 59. Identity, Access, and Tenant Administration. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Federate identities, provision roles, scope domains, enforce least privilege, manage privileged access, 
review entitlements, and revoke access across deployments.
Primary actors Customer identity administrators, security administrators, tenant and domain administrators, privileged-
access managers, auditors, and support.
Controlled product state Identity, organization, tenant, domain, role, policy, group, credential, session, device, workload, privilege, 
approval, recertification, revocation, and audit.
Human authority Administrators manage access but cannot grant themselves business decision authority, bypass 
separation of duty, or inspect customer data outside approved support purpose.
Primary failure condition Identity assurance fails, privileges conflict or become stale, federation is unavailable, revocation lags, or 
tenant scope is ambiguous.
Continuity contract Deny or reduce access, preserve existing low-risk sessions only by policy, expose impact, and invoke 
customer-controlled recovery.

60  Security, Cyber, and Incident Experience
Security, Cyber, and Incident Experience establishes the governed product capability to 
detect, investigate, contain, communicate, recover, and learn from security, privacy, 
integrity, availability, and supply-chain incidents without obscuring product state. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.

executives, legal, privacy, and auditors.
Controlled product state Finding, alert, incident, severity, scope, tenant, asset, evidence, timeline, action, containment, 
communication, decision, recovery, correction, and closure.
Human authority Automated response may execute pre-authorized containment; humans own incident command, material 
customer impact decisions, disclosure, exceptional access, and recovery acceptance.
Primary failure condition Detection is uncertain, scope expands, controls fail, evidence is compromised, responders lose access, or 
recovery would create unsafe state.
Continuity contract Contain the known boundary, preserve custody, declare uncertainty and degraded modes, escalate 
authority, and recover through verified reconciliation.

61  Model, Agent, and Data Governance Console
Model, Agent, and Data Governance Console establishes the governed product 
capability to inventory, evaluate, approve, constrain, monitor, change, suspend, roll 
back, and retire models, agents, datasets, prompts, tools, ontologies, and governed 
product packages. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, 
human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment 
profile.
Figure 62. Model, Agent, and Data Governance Console. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Inventory, evaluate, approve, constrain, monitor, change, suspend, roll back, and retire models, agents, 
datasets, prompts, tools, ontologies, and governed product packages.
Primary actors Model risk, agent governance, data governance, product owners, security, privacy, compliance, 
operations, domain authorities, and auditors.
Controlled product state Governed artifact, owner, version, purpose, risk class, dependencies, data, evaluation, policy, approval, 
deployment, monitoring, incident, exception, change, and lifecycle.
Human authority Governance automation assembles evidence and enforces gates; eligible humans approve risk, use, 
exceptions, releases, rollback, and retirement.
Primary failure condition Inventory or lineage is incomplete, evaluation expires, dependencies become unsafe, approval scope is 
exceeded, or runtime fitness degrades.
Continuity contract Block promotion or constrain running use, preserve exact state, expose affected capabilities, and route re-
evaluation or rollback.
Normative requirements

Audit, Evidence, Compliance, and Investigation establishes the governed product 
capability to search, preserve, verify, reconstruct, export, attest, and investigate 
attributable product, data, model, agent, administrative, and human decision evidence. 
It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 63. Audit, Evidence, Compliance, and Investigation. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human

Product contract

Product outcome Search, preserve, verify, reconstruct, export, attest, and investigate attributable product, data, model, 
agent, administrative, and human decision evidence.
Primary actors Internal audit, external audit, compliance, legal, security, privacy, regulators, customer assurance, 
investigators, and governance.
Controlled product state Audit event, actor, authority, action, object, version, reason, time, policy, correlation, digest, custody, 
retention, legal hold, case, export, and attestation.
Human authority Auditors and investigators receive purpose-bound access; operational administrators cannot alter, 
suppress, or certify evidence they control.

cannot verify.

investigation and remediation.

preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision

cannot prove reconciliation.

64  Integrations, APIs, Exports, and Extensibility
Integrations, APIs, Exports, and Extensibility establishes the governed product 
capability to integrate The Eye through versioned APIs, commands, events, 
subscriptions, data products, exports, imports, SDKs, and governed extensions without 
bypassing product controls. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer 
control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every 
deployment profile.
Figure 65. Integrations, APIs, Exports, and Extensibility. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human

Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Integrate The Eye through versioned APIs, commands, events, subscriptions, data products, exports, 
imports, SDKs, and governed extensions without bypassing product controls.
Primary actors Customer developers, integration engineers, data teams, partners, administrators, product owners, 
security, and governance.
Controlled product state Client, integration, contract, schema, credential, purpose, command, event, subscription, export, import, 
extension, version, quota, receipt, error, and lifecycle.
Human authority Integrations may invoke only authorized product contracts; external systems cannot write canonical state, 
synthesize approval, or strip provenance and policy.
Primary failure condition Contracts are incompatible, callers retry unsafely, exports exceed rights, imports fail validation, extensions 
are untrusted, or delivery is partial.
Continuity contract Reject or quarantine the operation, preserve receipts and checkpoints, expose partial state, and support 
deterministic retry, rollback, or reconciliation.

capability to receive one semantic product across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-
Premise, including approved disconnected and air-gapped profiles, with only declared 
operational variance. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer 
control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic product across every 
deployment profile.
Figure 66. SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise Product Parity. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled 
product state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Receive one semantic product across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise, including approved 
disconnected and air-gapped profiles, with only declared operational variance.
Primary actors Customers, product leadership, architecture, engineering, security, operations, commercial teams, 
implementation partners, and assurance.
Controlled product state Capability, deployment profile, version, provider choice, locality, service level, limitation, variance, fixture 
suite, evidence, exception, and parity status.
Human authority Deployment choice changes operational responsibility and topology, not human authority, object meaning, 
correction, explanation, export, replay, or core product behavior.
Primary failure condition A profile cannot pass common journeys, contracts, policies, failure, recovery, export, or replay fixtures.
Continuity contract Mark the capability unavailable or explicitly non-parity, prevent unsupported claims, and require 
remediation or controlled exception.

Product outcome Package and entitle capabilities, capacity, domains, agents, scenarios, data packages, integrations, and

renewal, suspension, evidence, and audit.

controls, or make the same named capability semantically different by deployment.

emergency operation is required.

unauthorized expansion, and reconcile commercial state.

CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Establish customer context, identity, policies, sources, data, ontology extensions, users, workflows, 
deployment, integrations, and acceptance through reversible governed onboarding.
Primary actors Customer sponsors, implementation leads, administrators, data owners, identity and security teams, 
domain experts, partners, and support.

validation, reconciliation, training, issue, approval, cutover, rollback, and acceptance.

unready, or cutover exceeds risk.

remediate by plan.

profile.

problem and change, and verify closure with the customer.
Identity, permission, purpose, policy, 
authority, approval, scope, or 
controlling version cannot be 
established.
Deny the consequential transition, preserve user work and context, expose the missing authority, 
and route accountable resolution.
A source, data product, model, 
agent, integration, network, or 
service dependency is stale, 
unavailable, indeterminate, or 
recovering.
Declare the product state and affected scope, constrain unsafe actions, retain evidence and 
checkpoints, and resume only after verified reconciliation.
Acceptance evidence
• End-to-end journey evidence demonstrates case and entitlement routing, secure evidence bundle, approved support 
access, SLA and escalation drills, known-error reuse, closure confirmation, and value review.
• Positive, negative, stale-state, unauthorized-role, cross-tenant, correction, and replay fixtures prove the product and 
authority contract.
• One golden journey suite passes across SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise with every permitted difference 
declared.
• A fault exercise proves the declared continuity response when support cannot reproduce the issue, access is 
unavailable, evidence is sensitive, commitments are missed, fixes regress, or recurring problems remain open, 
including visible state, audit, and recovery closure.
CHAPTER ACCEPTANCE AND INHERITANCE
Service Management, Support, and Customer Success is conformant only when every PR requirement closes for the 
declared acceptance unit, human authority is enforced, and product state remains truthful through correction, degradation, 
recovery, export, and replay. Primary anchors - V3: Ch. 1, Ch. 2; V4: Ch. 2, Ch. 3; V5: Ch. 2, Ch. 6; V6: Ch. 2, Ch. 3; V7: 
Ch. 2, Ch. 3. Full trace: Appendix N.

71  Product Acceptance and Operational Readiness
Product Acceptance and Operational Readiness establishes the governed product 
capability to accept a capability only when its user journeys, authority, contracts, data, 
AI, infrastructure, security, privacy, accessibility, performance, failure, recovery, support, 
and evidence are complete for declared scope. It preserves evidence, uncertainty, 
accountability, customer control, human decision sovereignty, and one semantic 
product across every deployment profile.
Figure 72. Product Acceptance and Operational Readiness. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product 
state, human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human 
authority, degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome
Accept a capability only when its user journeys, authority, contracts, data, AI, infrastructure, security, 
privacy, accessibility, performance, failure, recovery, support, and evidence are complete for declared 
scope.
Primary actors Customer sponsors, product authorities, architecture, engineering, security, privacy, data and AI 
governance, operations, accessibility, implementation, support, and independent assurance.
Controlled product state Acceptance unit, capability, journey, release, deployment, tenant or domain, data and model versions, 
consequence class, test suite, evidence, condition, exception, authority, expiry, and status.
Human authority No single team or automated score can self-accept the product; named independent and customer 
authorities approve their obligations and residual conditions.
Primary failure condition Evidence is missing or stale, scope is ambiguous, conditions expire, material defects remain, recovery is 
untested, or parity is unproven.
Continuity contract Withhold or limit acceptance, expose unmet obligations, preserve evidence, and require remediation and 
reacceptance before expansion.
Normative requirements

72  Product Governance and Baseline Closure
Product Governance and Baseline Closure establishes the governed product capability 
to govern the controlled PRD, resolve exceptions, measure conformance, approve 
amendments, retire capabilities, and preserve one coherent product baseline. It 
preserves evidence, uncertainty, accountability, customer control, human decision 
sovereignty, and one semantic product across every deployment profile.
Figure 73. Product Governance and Baseline Closure. The diagram binds product outcome, primary actors, controlled product state, 
human authority, primary failure condition, continuity contract into one governed product capability with explicit human authority, 
degraded behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Product contract
CONTRACT ELEMENT BINDING DEFINITION
Product outcome Govern the controlled PRD, resolve exceptions, measure conformance, approve amendments, retire 
capabilities, and preserve one coherent product baseline.
Primary actors Board and executive product authority, architecture and product councils, engineering, AI, infrastructure, 
data, security, privacy, operations, commercial leadership, and customer authorities.
Controlled product state Requirement, capability, owner, evidence, exception, risk acceptance, dependency, change proposal, 
impact, approval, effective time, release, migration, retirement, and supersession.
Human authority Local teams may implement within the baseline but cannot waive controlling meaning; constitutional or 
cross-volume change follows the recorded authority chain.
Primary failure condition Requirements conflict, exceptions age, evidence decays, local variants diverge, ownership lapses, or 
proposed change alters product identity.
Continuity contract Constrain affected release and use, make non-conformance visible, perform cross-volume impact analysis, 
and amend only through governing authority.

Capability conformance rules
RULE BINDING APPLICATION
Outcome before feature A capability exists to complete a governed user outcome; screens, APIs, models, agents, reports, and services are 
replaceable realization choices.
One accountable owner Every authoritative product lifecycle has one owner even where design, engineering, data, AI, operations, 
commercial, and customer duties are shared.
State is explicit Normal, draft, reviewed, approved, partial, stale, denied, disputed, quarantined, degraded, recovering, 
superseded, withdrawn, and retired states are not inferred from presentation.
Human authority is testable Every consequential transition names eligible authority, exact digest, required review, separation, expiry, intended 
effect, and audit evidence.
Acceptance follows exact 
scope
Conformance resolves to capability, journey, role, tenant or domain, deployment, entitlement, release, versions, 
configuration, consequence, period, conditions, and accepting authorities.
CATALOG AUTHORITY
The capability catalog freezes product responsibility and user outcome, not a screen map or vendor 
architecture. Volume 9 may design the experience only inside these identities, states, actions, 
authority boundaries, degraded behaviors, and acceptance contracts.

THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED PRODUCT BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 169
B  Persona, Role, and Authority Matrix
Personas express recurring goals and responsibilities. Roles grant policy-scoped 
permissions. Authority permits a defined institutional transition. A user may hold several 
roles, but the product never infers consequential authority from title, workspace access, 
administrative privilege, or AI recommendation.
ID PERSONA DECISION AUTHORITY PRIMARY PRODUCT 
RESPONSIBILITY
PROHIBITED 
SUBSTITUTION
PER-01 Board or governing 
body
Approves constitutional direction, 
strategic objectives, material risk 
appetite, and reserved decisions
Reviews strategic state, alternatives, 
commitments, and outcomes
Cannot delegate reserved 
authority to AI or 
administrators
PER-02 Executive decision 
authority
Makes accountable strategic 
decisions and commitments within 
recorded mandate
Reviews evidence, forecasts, scenarios, 
risk, dissent, and recommendations
Cannot approve a changed or 
incomplete decision digest
PER-03 Chief of staff or 
executive operator
Coordinates executive cadence, 
priorities, briefings, decisions, and 
follow-through
Curates agenda, routes work, tracks 
commitments, and escalates gaps
Cannot impersonate the 
decision authority
PER-04 Intelligence leader
Owns intelligence priorities, 
standards, coverage, review, and 
publication
Directs collection, analysis, warning, and 
briefing operations
Cannot waive source rights, 
truth state, or provenance
PER-05 Strategic analyst
Produces evidence-backed 
assessments, hypotheses, 
forecasts, scenarios, and decision 
support
Searches, curates, reasons, challenges, and 
publishes through review
Cannot convert an inference 
into approved fact
PER-06 Investigator
Builds attributable cases, 
timelines, entity networks, and 
evidence packages
Preserves custody, tests hypotheses, and 
documents limitations
Cannot exceed authorized 
purpose or conceal 
exculpatory evidence
PER-07 Forecaster
Owns forecast definition, 
methods, assumptions, 
calibration, and scoring
Produces and monitors probabilistic 
forecasts across horizons
Cannot publish unsupported 
precision or hide model 
disagreement
PER-08 Scenario or simulation 
specialist
Owns scenario framing, simulation 
validity, sensitivity, and 
reproducibility
Designs branches, models, runs, 
comparisons, and interpretation
Cannot mix observed and 
synthetic state
PER-09 Strategy and planning 
lead
Owns objective, capability, 
initiative, option, resource, and 
outcome alignment
Structures plans and decision cases and 
monitors execution
Cannot create commitments 
outside delegated authority
PER-10 Risk or opportunity 
owner
Accepts assessment, action, 
mitigation, sponsorship, and 
residual exposure
Reviews evidence, appetite, controls, 
options, and outcomes
Cannot leave material risk or 
opportunity unowned
PER-11 Collection manager
Owns source plans, contracts, 
coverage, priorities, and 
operational response
Approves sources and manages blind spots 
and incidents
Cannot infer rights from 
technical accessibility
PER-12 Data steward
Adjudicates identity, semantics, 
quality, correction, and lifecycle 
cases
Reviews merges, splits, mappings, 
corrections, and exceptions
Cannot silently alter historical 
evidence
PER-13 Knowledge engineer
Owns ontology, graph constraints, 
identity models, and semantic 
migration
Curates concepts, relationships, revisions, 
and projections
Cannot create domain forks of 
canonical meaning
PER-14 Model risk authority
Approves model use, evaluation, 
monitoring, exceptions, and 
retirement
Reviews data, fitness, calibration, safety, 
drift, and impact
Cannot self-approve a model 
they built
PER-15 Agent governor Approves agent role, tools, data, 
budgets, side effects, and lifecycle
Evaluates packages, permissions, runtime 
evidence, and incidents
Cannot grant human decision 
authority to an agent
PER-16 Security or privacy 
authority
Owns security, privacy, 
sovereignty, incident, and 
exception decisions
Reviews controls, purpose, locality, 
exposure, and evidence
Cannot broaden customer-
data access without authority
PER-17 Auditor or investigator 
of record
Independently verifies evidence, 
conformance, custody, and 
accountability
Reconstructs events, decisions, changes, 
incidents, and exceptions
Cannot alter the evidence 
being audited
PER-18 Tenant or domain 
administrator
Configures customer context, 
roles, domains, integrations, and 
policies
Provisions access and operates delegated 
customer controls
Cannot acquire business 
decision rights through 
administration
PER-19 Platform operator
Operates availability, capacity, 
change, recovery, and service 
health
Executes controlled operational procedures 
and evidence capture
Cannot inspect customer 
content without approved 
support purpose

meaning or authority.

WS-20 Product Delivery and

readiness

=== PAGE 172 ===
THE EYE VOLUME 8  /  PRODUCT REQUIREMENTS
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED PRODUCT BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 172
Navigation contract
NAVIGATION 
ELEMENT BINDING BEHAVIOR
Global operating context Shows and controls tenant, domain, role, objective, geography, horizon, scenario, classification, and deployment 
where applicable.
Workspace switch Preserves object identity, policy, active task, draft state, filter intent, provenance, and a deterministic return path.
Deep link Resolves an immutable object and version within current authority; denial reveals no protected metadata beyond 
policy.
History and back navigation Restores the verified prior context and never submits or repeats a command as a navigation side effect.
Command entry Separates navigation, query, draft, acknowledgment, review, approval, publication, and institutional effect.
Stale projection Displays source revision and watermark, offers the canonical path, and disables actions requiring fresher state.

Operational Readiness PR-71-001-PR-71-006 6
Accept a capability only when its user journeys, authority, 
contracts, data, AI, infrastructure, security, privacy, 
accessibility, performance, failure, recovery, support, and 
evidence are complete for declared scope.
C-004, C-035, C-042, 
C-043, C-049, C-052
72 Product Governance and 
Baseline Closure PR-72-001-PR-72-006 6
Govern the controlled PRD, resolve exceptions, measure 
conformance, approve amendments, retire capabilities, and 
preserve one coherent product baseline.
C-001, C-003, C-004, 
C-009, C-051, C-052
Requirement identity rules
RULE BINDING APPLICATION
Stable identity A PR identifier is never reused. Changed meaning receives controlled amendment, migration, impact analysis, and 
supersession evidence.
Atomic acceptance Each PR closes independently even where one test produces shared evidence for several requirements.
Bidirectional traceability Every PR resolves upward to authority and downward to journeys, design, components, data, AI, infrastructure, 
release, tests, operations, and retirement.
Runtime truth A passing build or historic test does not establish current conformance when configuration, data, model, agent, 
deployment, policy, evidence age, or operating state differs.

Acceptance record fields
FIELD BINDING CONTENT CLOSURE RULE
Acceptance unit Capability, journey, release, deployment, tenant or domain, role, entitlement, 
configuration, data and model versions, consequence, and period No implied adjacent scope
Evidence set Immutable test results, artifacts, telemetry, traces, reports, exceptions, conditions, 
and digests
Exact versions and dates 
resolve
Authorities Product, architecture, engineering, data, AI, security, privacy, accessibility, 
operations, commercial, independent, and customer roles as applicable Eligibility and separation verified
Conditions Residual risk, operational constraint, compensating control, owner, due date, 
expiry, and affected journey
Expiry constrains acceptance 
automatically
Decision Accepted, accepted with conditions, rejected, withdrawn, expired, or superseded Authenticated signature and 
rationale

THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED PRODUCT BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 186
G  Information Architecture and Object Action Catalog
The object-action model prevents presentation from inventing state changes. Every 
action resolves to an accountable object owner, permission, precondition, human gate, 
version, audit causation, correction path, and deterministic terminal state.
ID WORK 
OBJECT ACTION ELIGIBLE 
ACTOR PRODUCT EFFECT REQUIRED EVIDENCE
OBJ-01 Evidence item View original Any evidence 
consumer Never mutates source bytes Source, digest, rights, custody, time
OBJ-02 Evidence item Annotate Analyst or reviewer Creates a linked versioned 
annotation
Actor, purpose, target segment, 
time
OBJ-03 Evidence item Correct or 
withdraw Source authority Non-destructive governed case Authority, reason, target, impact
OBJ-04 Intelligence object Inspect Authorized consumer Shows canonical header and 
status Identity, version, time, truth, policy
OBJ-05 Intelligence object Propose 
assessment Analyst or agent Creates separate derived object Evidence, method, uncertainty, 
owner
OBJ-06 Entity Propose merge Steward or bounded 
agent No canonical effect before review Candidates, evidence, score, scope
OBJ-07 Entity Commit merge Eligible steward Versioned reversible canonical 
revision Approval, mapping, impact, audit
OBJ-08 Entity Split Eligible steward Preserves prior identity history Authority, reason, descendants, 
impact
OBJ-09 Relationship Propose Analyst or agent Remains a proposal until admitted Endpoints, type, time, evidence
OBJ-10 Relationship Adjudicate Steward or domain 
authority
Commits, rejects, or qualifies 
proposal Rationale, evidence, revision
OBJ-11 Claim Assert Authorized analyst or 
source adapter Explicit asserted truth state Proponent, evidence, scope, time
OBJ-12 Claim Assess Analyst, method, or 
agent Creates inference or assessment Method, confidence, uncertainty, 
dissent
OBJ-13 Claim Contest Authorized reviewer Preserves competing position Counter-evidence, rationale, actor
OBJ-14 Memory item Record Authorized knowledge 
owner
Creates permission-aware 
version Source, audience, validity, retention
OBJ-15 Memory item Retrieve Purpose-authorized 
user or agent No mutation; access is audited Purpose, version, policy decision
OBJ-16 Memory item Supersede Record authority Prior version remains replayable Reason, replacement, effective time
OBJ-17 Signal Nominate Detector, analyst, or 
agent Tentative until analyst disposition Baseline, deviation, sources, 
novelty
OBJ-18 Signal Disposition Eligible analyst Confirms, monitors, dismisses, or 
escalates Evidence, uncertainty, rationale
OBJ-19 Warning Issue Policy engine plus 
authorized owner Starts owned escalation lifecycle Trigger, severity, evidence, 
recipients
OBJ-20 Warning Acknowledge Named recipient Records receipt, not agreement Actor, time, intended response
OBJ-21 Warning Suppress Authorized owner Time-bound reasoned 
suppression Scope, reason, expiry, approval
OBJ-22 Risk Accept 
assessment Risk owner Accepts current exposure model Appetite, uncertainty, controls, 
review
OBJ-23 Opportunity Sponsor Eligible sponsor Creates owned evaluation or 
initiative Objective, option, budget, conditions
OBJ-24 Forecast Publish Forecaster and 
reviewer
Freezes cutoff, method, 
distribution Inputs, model, assumptions, horizon
OBJ-25 Forecast Supersede Forecast owner Prior forecast remains scoreable Reason, new version, effective time
OBJ-26 Scenario Branch Scenario author Creates explicit alternative future Parent, divergence, assumptions, 
owner
OBJ-27 Scenario package Install Customer package 
authority
No data or tool use before 
approval
Signature, rights, compatibility, 
scope
OBJ-28 Simulation Execute Authorized operator Uses exact immutable run 
manifest
Model, inputs, seed, constraints, 
budget

RULE REQUIRED BEHAVIOR
Evidence at the point of action A recipient can resolve trigger, evidence, uncertainty, affected objective or exposure, owner, time, correction 
state, and safe action without trusting notification text alone.
Acknowledgment is not 
agreement
Receipt, acknowledgment, review, acceptance, suppression, approval, action, and closure are separate 
attributable transitions.
Escalation is deterministic Recipient eligibility, delay, retry, redundant channel, substitute role, maximum time, and incident handoff are 
policy-controlled and testable.
Correction follows recipients Material corrections and withdrawals reach original recipient scope, link prior state, require acknowledgment 
where appropriate, and remain replayable.
Overload is a product failure Queues expose precision, duplication, suppression, delivery, ownership, backlog, severe-item visibility, and 
operator capacity rather than hiding volume.

Human decision action contract
CONTROL BINDING PRODUCT BEHAVIOR
Eligibility Resolve identity, role, delegation, separation, recusal, purpose, scope, time, device or session assurance, and 
required co-approval before presenting an enabled action.
Exact digest Bind the action to the complete question, options, evidence cutoff, versions, recommendation, dissent, conditions, 
intended effect, and current policy decision.
Deliberate action Do not infer approval from navigation, reading, acknowledgment, inactivity, chat text, default selection, bulk action, 
or administrator command.
Rationale and conditions Capture required rationale, reservations, conditions, owner, due time, review, and expiry according to 
consequence class.
Commit and receipt Persist human signature and decision before institutional effect; provide immutable receipt, created commitments, 
residual effects, and downstream status.
Invalidate on change Any material digest, authority, policy, evidence, option, recommendation, or intended-effect change cancels 
pending approval and requires review.

PHASE BINDING BEHAVIOR
Package creation Select authorized objects and dependencies; bind identity, policy, keys, revocations, cutoffs, expiry, provenance, 
schema, release, destination device or site, and digest.
Local operation Expose offline state and watermark; restrict queries and actions to package scope; persist encrypted attributable 
drafts, decisions, tool effects, and queued commands.
Reconnection Authenticate site and release, exchange revocations first, verify manifests, compare canonical versions, classify 
conflicts, and prevent blind last-writer-wins behavior.
Reconciliation Apply owner-specific merge or adjudication rules, preserve both histories, execute idempotent approved effects, 
rebuild projections, notify affected users, and retain receipts.
Closure Demonstrate canonical, derived, policy, audit, commitment, and customer-visible state convergence or retain an 
explicit unresolved degraded mode.

be removed or sold as optional safety features.

Marketplace package lifecycle
STAGE REQUIRED PRODUCT CONTRACT
Publish Verify publisher, source and build provenance, signature, rights, package identity, dependencies, data and tool 
requests, models, ontology, documentation, evaluation, support, and revocation.
Discover Expose purpose, domain, compatible releases, required entitlements, permissions, data use, locality, evaluation, 
known limits, price, publisher, and lifecycle.
Evaluate Run in an isolated non-production environment against customer-approved fixtures, policies, attack tests, 
accessibility, failure, and domain acceptance criteria.
Approve and install Require eligible customer and platform authorities; bind exact version, scope, tenant, domain, data, tools, model 
policy, budgets, deployment, and conditions.
Operate Monitor use, quality, drift, incidents, cost, policy, dependencies, updates, corrections, support, and customer-
visible product state.
Update or revoke Perform impact analysis, compatibility, re-evaluation, approval, cohort promotion, rollback, notification, evidence 
preservation, and safe retirement.

M  Product Analytics and Success Metric Catalog
Product analytics measures strategic-loop quality, evidence, human authority, 
observation, knowledge, foresight, decisions, agents, accessibility, reliability, adoption, 
economics, and customer outcomes. Metrics are governed data products; none 
authorizes surveillance, employee scoring, manipulation, or collection beyond approved 
purpose.

Metric governance
CONTROL BINDING REQUIREMENT
Contract Every metric declares purpose, definition, population, source, formula, dimensions, window, exclusions, quality, 
privacy class, owner, target, action, and retirement.
Balanced use No single metric represents strategic value, decision quality, employee performance, trust, or safety; leading, 
lagging, quality, outcome, cost, and harm indicators remain visible together.
Interpretation Coverage, confidence, seasonality, changes, cohort composition, deployment, entitlement, accessibility, and 
known bias accompany comparisons.
Action Thresholds create review or investigation workflows, not automatic business decisions or punitive action.
Audit Metric versions, source revisions, calculations, manual adjustments, exceptions, approvals, and uses remain 
replayable.

THE EYE VOLUME 8  /  PRODUCT REQUIREMENTS
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED PRODUCT BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 203
Constitutional Product Requirements matrix
ID INVARIANT BINDING STATEMENT PRIMARY VOLUME 8 
ANCHORS
C-001 Category integrity The Eye shall be conceived, designed, sold, and operated as a Strategic Intelligence Operating System. It 
shall not be reduced to a chatbot, dashboard, BI tool, copilot, or reporting product. Ch. 1, Ch. 2, Ch. 72
C-002 Closed strategic loop The product shall implement one continuous operating loop: Observe, Understand, Predict, Simulate, 
Decide, and Learn. No stage may be treated as a disconnected feature island. Ch. 2, Ch. 41, Ch. 72
C-003 Product boundary Interfaces such as chat, dashboards, reports, and APIs may exist, but they are access modes to the 
operating system rather than the product definition.
Ch. 1, Ch. 6, Ch. 8, Ch. 47, Ch. 64, Ch. 
72
C-004 Human decision sovereignty A named human authority shall make every final strategic or high-impact decision. The system may advise, 
prepare, and execute approved workflows, but it shall not silently assume decision rights.
Ch. 1, Ch. 2, Ch. 4, Ch. 7, Ch. 26, Ch. 
27, Ch. 28, Ch. 33, Ch. 37, Ch. 38, Ch. 
39, Ch. 40, Ch. 41, Ch. 42, Ch. 44, Ch. 
45, Ch. 46, Ch. 48, Ch. 49, Ch. 53, Ch. 
55, Ch. 56, Ch. 58, Ch. 60, Ch. 66, Ch. 
68, Ch. 71, Ch. 72
C-005 Explain every recommendation Every material forecast, scenario, simulation result, risk, opportunity, and recommendation shall expose 
evidence, assumptions, uncertainty, lineage, alternatives, and accountable system versions.
Ch. 2, Ch. 7, Ch. 11, Ch. 12, Ch. 21, 
Ch. 22, Ch. 23, Ch. 25, Ch. 26, Ch. 27, 
Ch. 28, Ch. 29, Ch. 32, Ch. 33, Ch. 35, 
Ch. 36, Ch. 38, Ch. 41, Ch. 42, Ch. 43, 
Ch. 44, Ch. 47, Ch. 52, Ch. 53, Ch. 55, 
Ch. 56, Ch. 57, Ch. 63
C-006 Enterprise operating standard The common platform shall satisfy enterprise requirements for governance, security, scale, auditability, 
reliability, and lifecycle management from its first production architecture.
Ch. 2, Ch. 3, Ch. 5, Ch. 31, Ch. 54, Ch. 
66, Ch. 67, Ch. 68
C-007 Universal core, governed 
specialization
A single domain-neutral core shall serve all customer classes. Domain ontologies, models, workflows, 
agents, and policies may extend the core without forking its constitutional semantics.
Ch. 3, Ch. 17, Ch. 31, Ch. 34, Ch. 54, 
Ch. 65
C-008 Ten-layer architecture The ten named architecture layers are the canonical product structure. Cross-cutting planes may support 
them but shall not replace, collapse, or rename them without constitutional amendment. Ch. 1, Ch. 5, Ch. 8, Ch. 41
C-009 Knowledge Graph centrality The Knowledge Graph shall be the connected semantic heart of The Eye and the shared context substrate 
for memory, twins, reasoning, prediction, scenarios, simulations, and decisions.
Ch. 18, Ch. 19, Ch. 29, Ch. 30, Ch. 31, 
Ch. 37, Ch. 51, Ch. 52, Ch. 72
C-010 Truth-state separation Observed evidence, extracted claims, inferred relationships, model outputs, human judgments, and 
synthetic scenario data shall remain explicitly distinguishable throughout the system.
Ch. 6, Ch. 11, Ch. 12, Ch. 16, Ch. 17, 
Ch. 18, Ch. 19, Ch. 20, Ch. 21, Ch. 23, 
Ch. 25, Ch. 29, Ch. 30, Ch. 32, Ch. 36, 
Ch. 43, Ch. 51, Ch. 52
C-011 Temporal truth Material facts and relationships shall preserve event time, observation time, validity time, and system-
record time so that past world states can be reconstructed without hindsight contamination.
Ch. 6, Ch. 11, Ch. 13, Ch. 14, Ch. 17, 
Ch. 19, Ch. 20, Ch. 21, Ch. 22, Ch. 24, 
Ch. 29, Ch. 30, Ch. 32, Ch. 36, Ch. 40, 
Ch. 62, Ch. 65
C-012 End-to-end provenance Every material intelligence object shall carry source, chain of custody, transformation history, ownership, 
rights, quality, and version lineage back to its evidence.
Ch. 6, Ch. 9, Ch. 11, Ch. 12, Ch. 13, 
Ch. 14, Ch. 16, Ch. 17, Ch. 18, Ch. 19, 
Ch. 20, Ch. 21, Ch. 22, Ch. 23, Ch. 24, 
Ch. 29, Ch. 40, Ch. 42, Ch. 43, Ch. 47, 
Ch. 50, Ch. 51, Ch. 52, Ch. 57, Ch. 62, 
Ch. 64, Ch. 65, Ch. 70, Ch. 8
C-013 Observation breadth The World Observation Layer shall support the full canonical source universe and allow governed custom 
sources without architectural rework. Ch. 9, Ch. 10, Ch. 15, Ch. 25, Ch. 50
C-014 Freshness and quality contracts Sources, derived data, twins, models, and intelligence products shall declare measurable freshness, 
completeness, quality, and expiry expectations.
Ch. 9, Ch. 10, Ch. 11, Ch. 12, Ch. 13, 
Ch. 14, Ch. 15, Ch. 16
C-015 Durable institutional memory The Eye shall preserve institutional context, decisions, assumptions, evidence, outcomes, and lessons as 
durable memory, subject to lawful retention, deletion, and customer policy. Ch. 20, Ch. 52
C-016 Memory governance Memory shall be permission-aware, tenant-isolated, versioned, retrievable, correctable, and protected from 
unverified model output becoming accepted organizational truth. Ch. 12, Ch. 18, Ch. 20, Ch. 24
C-017 Strategy Graph Objectives, hypotheses, decisions, initiatives, capabilities, resources, indicators, risks, opportunities, and 
outcomes shall be connected in a governed Strategy Graph anchored to the Knowledge Graph. Ch. 15, Ch. 28, Ch. 37, Ch. 45
C-018 Grounded Digital Twins Every Digital Twin shall be grounded in evidence, graph entities, temporal state, declared assumptions, 
and calibrated models; it shall never present synthetic state as observed fact. Ch. 30, Ch. 35, Ch. 36
C-019 Twin versionability Digital Twins shall support time travel, branching, comparison, replay, reconciliation, and versioned 
evolution across actual and simulated states. Ch. 35, Ch. 36
C-020 Canonical prediction horizons The Prediction Engine shall support 30-day, 90-day, 180-day, 1-year, 3-year, and 5-year horizons with 
horizon-appropriate methods and uncertainty. Ch. 32, Ch. 53
C-021 Probabilistic forecasting Forecasts shall communicate distributions, confidence, calibration, drivers, sensitivity, and failure 
conditions rather than false precision or unsupported certainty.
Ch. 21, Ch. 25, Ch. 27, Ch. 28, Ch. 30, 
Ch. 32, Ch. 38
C-022 Scenario plurality Scenario Intelligence shall maintain multiple coherent futures, including baseline, upside, downside, 
disruption, and user-defined alternatives; no single forecast shall masquerade as destiny. Ch. 33, Ch. 35, Ch. 38, Ch. 53
C-023 Reproducible simulation Every simulation shall declare initial state, assumptions, interventions, model versions, constraints, random 
seeds where applicable, outputs, and sensitivity so results can be reproduced and challenged. Ch. 35, Ch. 36, Ch. 38, Ch. 53
C-024 Decision completeness Decision Intelligence shall compare viable options against explicit objectives, constraints, risks, 
opportunities, trade-offs, second-order effects, reversibility, and information value.
Ch. 27, Ch. 33, Ch. 37, Ch. 38, Ch. 39, 
Ch. 45
C-025 Decision Replay The Eye shall preserve the state of knowledge available at decision time and support replay against later 
evidence and outcomes without rewriting the historical record. Ch. 22, Ch. 24, Ch. 39, Ch. 40
C-026 Objective alignment Recommendations shall be evaluated against declared objectives, stakeholder obligations, risk appetite, 
policy, and strategic constraints rather than an opaque global score.
Ch. 15, Ch. 27, Ch. 28, Ch. 33, Ch. 37, 
Ch. 41, Ch. 43, Ch. 45
C-027 Executive Operating System The executive surface shall organize strategic attention, decisions, scenarios, evidence, objectives, 
commitments, and outcomes. It shall not degrade into a passive dashboard collection. Ch. 41, Ch. 42, Ch. 45
C-028 Bounded agent authority Every agent shall have a declared purpose, tools, data scope, permissions, budgets, escalation rules, 
prohibited actions, evaluation suite, and accountable owner.
Ch. 4, Ch. 23, Ch. 39, Ch. 46, Ch. 49, 
Ch. 50, Ch. 51, Ch. 52, Ch. 53, Ch. 54, 
Ch. 55, Ch. 56, Ch. 61
C-029 Supervised orchestration Planner, Supervisor, and Workflow agents shall enforce policy, separation of duties, checkpoints, timeouts, 
retries, human gates, and complete execution traces across multi-agent work.
Ch. 4, Ch. 23, Ch. 46, Ch. 49, Ch. 50, 
Ch. 55, Ch. 56
C-030 Model pluralism The platform shall support multiple models and reasoning approaches behind governed interfaces so 
customers retain control over cost, sovereignty, performance, and vendor dependence. Ch. 31, Ch. 54, Ch. 61, Ch. 49

C-031 No silent self-modification Continuous learning shall never permit unreviewed production models, agents, prompts, policies, 
ontologies, or decision logic to modify themselves silently. Ch. 40, Ch. 51, Ch. 61
C-032 Proactive intelligence The system shall surface material changes, weak signals, risks, and opportunities before users ask, while 
respecting relevance, authority, attention, and notification policy.
Ch. 10, Ch. 15, Ch. 25, Ch. 26, Ch. 28, 
Ch. 29, Ch. 30, Ch. 44, Ch. 55
C-033 Early-warning discipline Warnings shall be deduplicated, prioritized, evidence-backed, confidence-aware, time-bounded, and linked 
to affected objectives, assets, decisions, and response playbooks. Ch. 10, Ch. 13, Ch. 26, Ch. 44
C-034 Decomposable Strategic Health 
Score
Any Strategic Health Score shall expose component measures, weights, evidence, confidence, trend, and 
sensitivity. The score shall never replace the underlying analysis. Ch. 37, Ch. 43, Ch. 70
C-035 Trust as a system property Trust, provenance, identity, authorization, policy, safety, and audit shall operate across every layer and 
intelligence object, not as a downstream compliance add-on.
Ch. 8, Ch. 9, Ch. 16, Ch. 17, Ch. 18, 
Ch. 19, Ch. 21, Ch. 22, Ch. 31, Ch. 34, 
Ch. 49, Ch. 54, Ch. 56, Ch. 57, Ch. 58,

Ch. 64, Ch. 66, Ch. 69, Ch. 71
C-036 Non-repudiable accountability Material access, transformations, agent actions, approvals, decisions, overrides, releases, and exports 
shall produce tamper-evident audit records attributable to identities and versions.
Ch. 4, Ch. 6, Ch. 18, Ch. 22, Ch. 24, 
Ch. 26, Ch. 27, Ch. 39, Ch. 42, Ch. 44, 
Ch. 45, Ch. 46, Ch. 47, Ch. 56, Ch. 57, 
Ch. 58, Ch. 60, Ch. 62, Ch. 69
C-037 Customer data control Customers retain governance over their data, models, twins, policies, retention, residency, encryption, 
exports, and deletion within the chosen deployment and contractual boundary.
Ch. 3, Ch. 9, Ch. 14, Ch. 20, Ch. 48, 
Ch. 58, Ch. 59, Ch. 67, Ch. 69, Ch. 70
C-038 Open interoperability The Eye shall integrate through governed APIs, events, connectors, common data formats, and export 
paths; core customer knowledge shall not be trapped behind proprietary-only access.
Ch. 14, Ch. 34, Ch. 47, Ch. 57, Ch. 64, 
Ch. 65, Ch. 67
C-039 Zero-trust security Identity, device, workload, agent, model, source, and data access shall be continuously authenticated, 
authorized, scoped, logged, and re-evaluated under least privilege.
Ch. 13, Ch. 16, Ch. 34, Ch. 48, Ch. 50, 
Ch. 58, Ch. 59, Ch. 60, Ch. 64
C-040 Privacy and minimization Collection and use shall be purpose-bound, proportionate, policy-enforced, and minimized, with support for 
consent, legal basis, access, correction, retention, and deletion obligations.
Ch. 3, Ch. 9, Ch. 14, Ch. 15, Ch. 16, 
Ch. 50, Ch. 59, Ch. 62, Ch. 70
C-041 Sovereignty by design Data residency, key ownership, network isolation, local inference, model choice, administrative control, and 
disconnected operation shall be supported where customer policy requires them. Ch. 3, Ch. 48, Ch. 58, Ch. 59, Ch. 65
C-042 Deployment semantic parity
SaaS, Private Cloud, and On-Premise deployments shall preserve the same core semantics, governance 
model, APIs, artifacts, and intelligence lifecycle, with documented capability differences only where 
unavoidable.
Ch. 3, Ch. 8, Ch. 48, Ch. 63, Ch. 65, 
Ch. 66, Ch. 67, Ch. 71
C-043 Resilient operation The platform shall fail safely, degrade intelligibly, recover predictably, preserve evidence and audit 
continuity, and expose when data, models, or dependencies are unavailable or stale.
Ch. 10, Ch. 13, Ch. 24, Ch. 46, Ch. 48, 
Ch. 55, Ch. 60, Ch. 63, Ch. 67, Ch. 69, 
Ch. 71
C-044 Governed learning loop Learning from outcomes, feedback, overrides, and new evidence shall pass through evaluation, approval, 
release, monitoring, rollback, and tenant-isolation controls. Ch. 40, Ch. 72
C-045 Continuous evaluation Models, agents, forecasts, extractors, ontologies, simulations, and recommendations shall be evaluated for 
accuracy, calibration, robustness, drift, bias, safety, cost, and operational fitness.
Ch. 12, Ch. 23, Ch. 25, Ch. 32, Ch. 35, 
Ch. 43, Ch. 51, Ch. 61, Ch. 70, Ch. 36
C-046 Governed marketplaces Scenario and Agent Marketplace packages shall be signed, versioned, inspectable, permission-scoped, 
licensed, evaluated, sandboxed, monitored, revocable, and traceable to publishers. Ch. 34, Ch. 49, Ch. 61, Ch. 64, Ch. 66
C-047 Experience integrity The product experience shall expose context, uncertainty, provenance, decision state, and consequence at 
the point of use; critical meaning shall not be hidden behind ornamental visualization.
Ch. 2, Ch. 5, Ch. 7, Ch. 11, Ch. 17, Ch. 
19, Ch. 33, Ch. 41, Ch. 42, Ch. 44, Ch. 
47, Ch. 57, Ch. 63, Ch. 68, Ch. 69
C-048 Role and accessibility clarity Experiences shall be accessible and role-appropriate for executives, analysts, operators, domain experts, 
administrators, and governance teams without creating separate truths. Ch. 4, Ch. 5, Ch. 7, Ch. 46, Ch. 68
C-049 Operational quality contract Reliability, performance, scale, freshness, accuracy, calibration, explainability, reproducibility, security, 
accessibility, and cost shall be measured as product behavior, not assumed as implementation detail.
Ch. 5, Ch. 6, Ch. 7, Ch. 10, Ch. 26, Ch. 
60, Ch. 62, Ch. 63, Ch. 66, Ch. 67, Ch. 
68, Ch. 69, Ch. 70, Ch. 71
C-050 Responsible-use boundary The Eye shall not autonomously execute decisions that materially determine life, liberty, legal status, 
access to essential services, employment, credit, or equivalent high-impact rights and interests.
Ch. 4, Ch. 7, Ch. 31, Ch. 34, Ch. 39, 
Ch. 49, Ch. 54, Ch. 59, Ch. 68
C-051 Constitutional change control Changes to mission, product category, human authority, principles, canonical layers, core semantics, or 
constitutional invariants require explicit amendment, impact analysis, ratification, and versioned publication. Ch. 1, Ch. 8, Ch. 65, Ch. 72
C-052 Documentation inheritance Volumes 1 through 10 shall inherit this constitution, use its canonical terms, trace material requirements to 
invariant IDs, and identify rather than conceal any unresolved conflict. Ch. 1, Ch. 5, Ch. 8, Ch. 71, Ch. 72

P  Product Glossary
These terms are canonical for product behavior, human authority, observation, 
knowledge, memory, foresight, scenarios, simulation, decisions, agents, trust, 
operations, delivery, and acceptance. Volume 9 and domain specifications may add 
narrower terms but may not redefine the concepts below.
TERM PRODUCT DEFINITION
Abstention Explicit product state in which a model, agent, or method declines to assert an answer because 
evidence, authority, fitness, or scope is insufficient.
Acceptance unit Exact capability, journey, release, deployment, tenant or domain, role, versions, configuration, 
consequence class, and period being accepted.
Agent Governed software principal with identity, role, instructions, tools, data scopes, budgets, evaluation, 
runtime trace, and bounded authority.
Agent Marketplace Controlled catalog and lifecycle for publishing, evaluating, approving, installing, operating, updating, 
and retiring agent packages.
Assessment Versioned human or machine judgment linked to evidence, method, truth state, confidence, 
uncertainty, dissent, and accountable owner.
Authority Recorded right of an eligible human or governing body to approve a defined transition or institutional 
effect within explicit scope and time.
Blind spot Known or suspected absence of observation coverage across a declared entity, geography, source, 
topic, population, period, or quality dimension.
Capability Stable product responsibility delivering a governed user outcome through explicit state, authority, 
contracts, failure behavior, and acceptance evidence.
Canonical object Governed intelligence object whose identity, version, time, truth state, provenance, policy, quality, 
correction, owner, and lifecycle follow the universal contract.
Claim Explicit proposition attributed to a source or actor and kept distinct from supporting evidence, 
inference, assessment, recommendation, and decision.
Closed strategic loop Observe, Understand, Predict, Simulate, Decide, and Learn as one continuous governed operating 
cycle.
Collection contract Versioned authority, rights, purpose, scope, endpoint, schedule, coverage, freshness, schema, cost, 
withdrawal, and evidence agreement for a source.
Commitment Accountable institutional obligation created by human authority with owner, due time, conditions, 
evidence, status, exceptions, and outcome.
Consequence class Governed category determining required evidence, review, human gate, controls, service objectives, 
and acceptance for an action or output.
Contestability Ability of an authorized person to challenge evidence, method, interpretation, model, 
recommendation, policy decision, or product state and obtain accountable disposition.
Correction Non-destructive authority-backed change that updates decision-active state while preserving prior 
versions, knowledge, use, and downstream impact.
Customer operating context Tenant, domain, organization, jurisdiction, deployment, purpose, policy, data authority, specialization, 
and responsibility configuration for a customer.
Decision Final accountable choice made by an eligible authenticated human against an exact decision digest.
Decision case Governed work object containing question, objectives, options, evidence, assumptions, foresight, risk, 
opportunity, recommendation, dissent, review, and authority.
Decision digest Immutable cryptographic identity of the exact decision package presented for human approval.
Decision intelligence Product capability that structures alternatives, evidence, foresight, trade-offs, uncertainty, 
recommendation, and explanation for human decision-makers.
Decision Replay Reconstruction of decision-time evidence, versions, methods, recommendations, authority, rationale, 
commitments, later outcomes, and corrections without rewriting history.
Degraded mode Declared customer-visible state that names unavailable or constrained behavior, affected scope, 
freshness, safe actions, owner, maximum duration, and exit evidence.
Digital Twin Governed time-versioned representation of a company, competitor, supply chain, product, market, 
regulation, infrastructure, process, or organization.
=== PAGE 208 ===
THE EYE VOLUME 8  /  PRODUCT REQUIREMENTS
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED PRODUCT BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 208
TERM PRODUCT DEFINITION
Dissent Preserved evidence-backed disagreement that remains visible through assessment, review, decision, 
correction, and replay.
Domain Governed customer or intelligence boundary that specializes sources, ontology, workflows, methods, 
policy, and products without forking the universal core.
Early warning Governed explainable notice that a threshold or pattern relevant to objectives or exposure requires 
named human attention within a defined time.
Enterprise Memory Permission-aware temporal institutional record of facts, artifacts, context, decisions, rationales, 
commitments, outcomes, and lessons.
Entitlement Commercially governed right for a tenant or user to access a capability, capacity, package, 
environment, or service under explicit limits and lifecycle.
Evidence Source-grounded material with identity, version, integrity, rights, custody, time, provenance, policy, 
quality, and lifecycle.
Explainability Product ability to show evidence, method, assumptions, alternatives, uncertainty, provenance, 
limitations, authority, and expected effect at the point of use.
Forecast Probabilistic estimate of a defined target over a stated horizon using an exact cutoff, method, model, 
assumptions, distribution, calibration state, and owner.
Governed learning Evidence-driven proposal to change product, policy, process, model, agent, data, or configuration 
through evaluation, human approval, controlled release, and replay.
Human authority boundary Explicit line separating AI recommendation, forecast, simulation, reasoning, and explanation from 
human approval, decision, commitment, and risk acceptance.
Indicator Versioned measurable condition linked to source data, formula, owner, threshold, coverage, objective 
or exposure, and lifecycle.
Intelligence object Typed product object representing evidence, entity, relationship, event, claim, assessment, forecast, 
scenario, simulation, decision, commitment, or outcome.
Knowledge Graph Canonical temporal semantic system connecting entities, relationships, events, claims, evidence, 
objectives, capabilities, risks, and strategic products.
Marketplace package Signed versioned agent, scenario, data, ontology, integration, or domain extension with publisher, 
rights, dependencies, evaluation, permissions, compatibility, and lifecycle.
Material journey End-to-end user outcome whose failure can affect human authority, strategic meaning, customer 
control, safety, rights, operations, or acceptance.
Objective Governed desired strategic outcome with owner, measures, targets, capabilities, dependencies, 
options, risks, commitments, and time horizon.
Observation Time-bound source-grounded record of something detected or received, kept distinct from 
interpretation and assessment.
Offline package Signed encrypted bounded set of product state, data, policy, identity, revocation, expiry, and 
synchronization instructions for disconnected use.
Operating context Active tenant, domain, role, objective, geography, horizon, scenario, classification, and deployment 
scope governing a product interaction.
Opportunity Evidence-backed strategic hypothesis with value range, timing, dependencies, required capabilities, 
risks, sponsor, options, and outcome.
Outcome Observed result linked to decisions, commitments, interventions, context, measures, uncertainty, and 
cautious attribution.
Product parity Preservation of user outcomes, objects, authority, explanation, correction, export, replay, failure, and 
acceptance semantics across deployment profiles.
Product state Explicit current condition of a capability or object, including normal, partial, stale, denied, quarantined, 
disputed, degraded, recovering, superseded, or retired.
Proactive intelligence Governed nomination and prioritization of material warnings, decisions, reviews, opportunities, gaps, 
commitments, and exceptions before manual inquiry.
Provenance Attributable chain connecting source, actor, method, model, tool, policy, transformation, version, 
review, approval, release, delivery, correction, and output.
Recommendation Explainable non-authoritative proposal linked to objectives, alternatives, evidence, assumptions, 
forecasts, scenarios, simulations, risks, uncertainty, and expected effects.
Release Immutable dependency-closed set of product, code, configuration, policies, schemas, ontologies, 
data, models, agents, packages, documentation, and evidence.
Residual effect Institutional or technical effect that remains after cancellation, failure, compensation, rollback, 
containment, or override.

THE EYE VOLUME 8  /  PRODUCT REQUIREMENTS
THE EYE  /  CONTROLLED PRODUCT BASELINE V1.0 PAGE 209
TERM PRODUCT DEFINITION
Risk Uncertain condition or event that can affect objectives, expressed through cause, consequence, 
likelihood distribution, exposure, owner, appetite, controls, mitigation, and residual state.
Scenario Coherent plausible future with explicit drivers, uncertainties, assumptions, branches, indicators, 
implications, options, evidence, and owner.
Scenario Marketplace Controlled catalog and lifecycle for publishing, discovering, licensing, installing, evaluating, updating, 
and retiring scenario packages.
Simulation Reproducible governed execution of a model against exact inputs, twin or scenario state, parameters, 
constraints, seeds, versions, and validity rules.
Source coverage Measured observation reach across declared entities, regions, topics, populations, periods, source 
classes, and quality dimensions.
Strategic Health Score Decomposable governed assessment of strategic health whose measures, lineage, weights, 
confidence, coverage, trends, and exceptions remain visible.
Strategic package Versioned forecast, scenario, simulation, decision case, briefing, or other decision-support product 
bound to exact evidence and authority.
Strategy Graph Governed semantic graph connecting objectives, outcomes, measures, capabilities, initiatives, 
resources, dependencies, risks, opportunities, decisions, commitments, and results.
Strategic record Durable institutional record of a material assessment, decision, rationale, commitment, exception, 
outcome, or lesson.
Tenant Top-level customer isolation and governance boundary for identity, data, policy, workloads, keys, 
operations, support, evidence, and lifecycle.
Temporal truth Preservation of event time, observation time, validity interval, system-record time, version, correction, 
and decision-time availability.
Truth state Explicit epistemic category such as observed, asserted, inferred, assessed, synthetic, recommended, 
decided, corrected, withdrawn, or unknown.
Uncertainty Explicit limits arising from evidence, coverage, measurement, model, assumptions, future variability, 
disagreement, or unknowns.
Warning owner Named accountable person or role responsible for acknowledgment, disposition, escalation, action, 
correction, and closure of a warning.
Weak signal Low-strength or emerging evidence pattern nominated for governed evaluation, not a confirmed fact 
or warning.
Work object Stable product object with identity, owner, version, lifecycle, policy, tasks, authority, provenance, 
correction, audit, and replay behavior.
Workspace Role-appropriate operating environment that organizes governed work objects, navigation, context, 
queues, actions, explanations, collaboration, and state.
Baseline closure
Volume 8 closes when every production capability can identify its customer and user outcome, 
accountable owner, workspace, journey, work objects, authority, lifecycle, evidence, time, truth state, 
provenance, uncertainty, explanation, correction, accessibility, localization, deployment profile, 
entitlement, dependencies, product state, failure behavior, offline behavior, recovery, success metrics, 
acceptance unit, evidence, conditions, exception, release, support path, and retirement state.
END STATE
The Eye is product-complete here as one enterprise Strategic Intelligence Operating System: 
continuously observing, semantically understanding, institutionally remembering, probabilistically 
predicting, reproducibly simulating, explainably recommending, durably operating, and learning 
through governed evidence while humans retain final decision authority in every deployment.
